patippurai

tamiḻmoḻiyiṉ taṉit taṉmai, toṉmai, muṉmai, teṉmai, ciṟappu ākiyavaṟṟaip paṟṟi moḻiyiyalaṟiñarum piṟa tuṟaiyaṟiñarum kūṟiyuḷḷavaṟṟuḷ ciṟantavaṟṟait tokuttut tarumāṟu namatu patippaka nūlāciriyarkaḷuḷ oruvarāṉa aṟiñar pi.irāmanātaṉ avarkaḷaik kēṭṭuk koṇṭēṉ, avarum tokuttut tantuḷḷār. karuttukkaḷait toṭarpupaṭuttik kāṭṭum vakaiyil āṅkāṅkē cila viḷakkaṅkaḷaiyum tantuḷḷār. paltuṟai aṟiviyalpulaṅkaḷ cārnta vallunarkaḷ evar muṉṉilaiyilum iṉṟu nilaināṭṭakkūṭiya karutturaikaḷē tarappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. kūṟiyatu kūṟalāka vantavaiyum, veṟum mukamaṉukkāka kūṟappaṭṭavaiyum viṭappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. mukkiyamāṉavaṟṟaiyellām yār eṅku eppoḻutu ceppiyatu eṉṟa vivaraṅkaḷ tarappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. ( āṅkila nūlkaḷ, itaḻkaḷ ākiyavaṟṟiliruntu eṭuttavaṟṟaip poṟuttavarai avaṟṟiṉ peyarkaḷ āṅkilattilēyē tarap paṭṭuḷḷaṉa -- mūlattait tēṭi mēlum payila virumpuvār nalaṉ karuti). ic ceytikaḷuḷ perumpālāṉavai ēṟkaṉavē namatu patippakam veḷiyiṭṭuḷḷa “toṉmaic cemmoḻi tamiḻ” (2007) “tamiḻar varalāṟu: iṉṟai nōkkil - paṇṭu mutal iṉṟu varai ” (2008) ākiya nūlkaḷiliruntu tokukkappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. cila putiya ceytikaḷum iṭam peṟukiṉṟaṉa. iṇaippukaḷ I, II ākiyavai cārnta āṅkila mūlaṅkaḷukku itu varait tamiḻākkaṅkaḷ evaiyum vantatākat teriyavillai. ippoḻutu mutaṉ mutalāka varukiṉṟaṉa eṉalām.

2. cila iṭaṅkaḷil (e.kā: oru pāl tamiḻukkum maṟupāl ulakiluḷḷa ērāḷamāṉa moḻikaḷ, palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷ ākiyavaṟṟukkum iṭaiyil uḷḷa uṟavu paṟṟiya nūṟṟukkaṇakkāṉa aṟiñarkaḷ nūlkaḷaip poṟuttavarai avarkaḷ peyarp paṭṭiyalait tara maṭṭumē iṭamikuntatu. ivvivarattait tarum ulakappaṭam oṉṟum iyal 2- il uḷḷatu.

3. caṅka ilakkiyac ciṟappup paṟṟiya aṟiñar karuttukkaḷ iyal 4il tarappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. icceytikaḷ moḻiyiyal cārntavaiyaṉṟeṉiṉum innūlaip paṭippavarkaḷ payaṉkarutic cērkkappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa;

4. innūlai uruvākkiya aṟiñar pi. irāmanātaṉ avarkaḷukku eṉ naṉṟiyaitterivittuk koḷkiṟēṉ.

II. 		hoyciṅṭaṉ 1853 l
“kīḻtticaiyiyal āyvukaḷukkāṉa
amerikka caṅka āyvitaḻil”
(Journal of the American Oriental
Society Vol -3)  veḷiyiṭṭa tamiḻ
moḻi paṟṟiya kaṭṭurai -
tamiḻākkamum āṅkila mūlamum 						    166


iyal – 1

tamiḻiya moḻik kuṭumpam

1. pirāṉcis vaiṭ ellis

(1816) il tamiḻiya (tirāviṭa) moḻik kuṭumpam taṉiyāṉatu eṉṟu mutalil niṟuviyavar)

“tamiḻō, teluṅkō avaṟṟiṉ iṉamoḻikaḷō camaṟkirutattiliruntu kiḷaittu uruvāṉavai alla; camaṟkirutam immoḻikaḷai merukūṭṭap payaṉpaṭuttap
paṭṭirunta pōtilum avaṟṟiṉ iruppukkut (nilaippukkut) tēvaiyaṟṟatu; avai oru taṉiyāṉa moḻik kuṭumpattaic cārntavai. ammoḻikaḷil camaṟkirutac coṟkaḷ (kuṟippākap piṟkālaṅkaḷil) kalantuḷḷa pōtilum vērccoṟkaḷ nilaiyil avaṟṟōṭu camaṟkiruttukkut toṭarpillai. 

-Francis Whyte Ellis (1816): “Note to the introduction” (31pages) in A Grammar of the Teloogoo language by A.D.Campbell; Thomas R.Trautmann (2007): Languages and Nations : “The Dravidian proof in Colonial Madras” tamiḻil irāma.cuntaram(2007) “tirāviṭac cāṉṟu: ellisum
tirāviṭa moḻikaḷum” ceṉṉai vaḷarcci – ārāycci niṟuvaṉam ceṉṉai; maṟṟum kālaccuvaṭu patippakam; pak.340. ellis kuṟippiṉ tamiḻākkam iṇaippu I l uḷḷatu. (ataṉ āṅkila vaṭivam - mūlattiṉ niḻaṟpaṭi - “tirāviṭaccāṉṟu”) iṟutiyil tarappaṭṭuḷḷatu.

2. peñcamiṉ kai pāpiṅṭaṉ (1830)

paḻantamiḻ eḻuttukkaḷaip paṟṟik kūṟum poḻutu avaṟṟiṉ mikunta eḷimaiyait tāṉ nāṉ mutalil kuṟippiṭa vēṇṭum. anta eḷimaiyum vēṟu cila taṉmaikaḷum tām tamiḻmoḻi aḷaviṟanta toṉmai vāyntatu eṉpatai niṟuvukiṉṟaṉa. tamiḻi varivaṭivattiliruntu (lipiyiliruntu) uruvākkappaṭṭa kiranta lipiyil tāṉ teṉṉintiyāvil camaṟkirutam eḻutappaṭukiṟatu; kiranta lipi cikkalāṉatāka iruppatē atu piṟkālattil uruvākkappaṭṭirukka vēṇṭum eṉpataṟkuc cāṉṟākum. tamiḻmoḻi camaṟkirutattiliruntu muṟṟilum māṟupaṭṭa moḻiyamaippaik koṇṭatu; kaṉaittu olikkum (aspirated) meykaḷ illai; camaṟkiruta neṭuṅkaṇakkil illāta olikaḷum eḻuttukkaḷum tamiḻil uḷḷaṉa. tamiḻil uḷḷa pala vaḻakkumoḻikaḷil oṉṟil (centamiḻil) camaṟkirutac coṟkaḷ mikaccilavē; tamiḻ intiyāviṉ teṉkōṭip pakutiyil iruppatu oṉṟē tamiḻ tāṉāka uruvākiyatu eṉpataiyum kuṟaintapaṭcam “camaṟkirutam uruvāṉa kālak kaṭṭattilēyē uruvāṉa toṉmai uṭaiyatu atu” eṉpataiyum kāṭṭuvatākum. ivviṣayam ikkaṭṭuraip poruḷaic cārntatalla; itaip paṟṟi mēlum neṭiya viḷakkam aḷittiṭa iṅku iṭamillai.

- Benjamin Guy Babrngton(1830) An account of the sculptures and inscriptions at Mahamalaipur TRANSACTIONS OF THE ROYAL ASIATIC SOCIETY.Vol II (Paper read on 12.07.1828)

3. heṉṟi hoyciṅṭaṉ (1853)

centamiḻai viṭac ceṟivu, colvaḷam, ekkaruttaiyum veḷiyiṭum āṟṟal, iṉimai ākiya taṉmaikaḷaik koṇṭa moḻi vēṟu etuvum irukka iyalātu. 

teṉṉintiyāvil mottam ēṟattāḻa iraṇṭu mūṉṟu kōṭi pēr pēcum teluṅku, kaṉṉaṭam, malaiyāḷam, tuḷuvam maṟṟum cila moḻikaḷait tōṟṟuvittatu tamiḻē eṉṟu karutappaṭukiṟatu;ākavē tamiḻait teṉṉintiyat (toṉ) moḻiyākavē karutalām.

-Reverend Henry Hoisington (1853) : Brief notes on the Tamil Language. JOURNAL OF THE AMERICAN ORIENTAL SOCIETY III Article (ix) (Paper read on 19.05.1852) itaṉ muḻumaiyāṉa tamiḻākkam iṇaippu II l.



4. ji.yū.pōp (1855)

“(tamiḻiya, atāvatu tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ) camaskirutam tōṉṟiya kālattil tōṉṟiyatum camaṟkirutam enta muṉmoḻiyiliruntu tōṉṟiyatō atē muṉmoḻiyi
liruntē tōṉṟiyatum āṉa oru moḻiyiliruntu uruvākip piṉ pirintavai ākum. antat tamiḻiya moḻikaḷukkum (intō āriya moḻik kuṭumpattaic cārnta) kirīk, kātik, pāracīkam mutaliya moḻikaḷukkum iṭaiyē oppumai iruntataik kāṭṭum pala cāṉṟukaḷ uḷḷaṉa. camaṟkirut tattukkum tamiḻiya moḻikaḷukkum oppumai illāta nērvukaḷilum kūṭa attakaiya oppumaikaḷ uḷḷaṉa.)” 

- tamiḻ moḻik kaiyēṭu, A Hand book of Tamil Language 1855.

5. irāparṭ kālṭuvel (1856/1875): tirāviṭa allatu teṉṉintiya moḻikaḷiṉ oppilakkaṇam

nūliṉ muṉṉuraiyiliruntu

tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiṉ ilakkaṇaṅkaḷai oppunōkki muṟaiyāṉa āḻnta aṟiviyal pūrvamāṉa āyvai uruvākkuvatē innūlil eṉatu mukkiyamāṉa nōkka mākum. ammoḻikaḷukkum piṟamoḻikaḷukkum uḷḷa uṟavukaḷ paṟṟi iṉṟu palvēṟu karutukōḷkaḷ uḷḷaṉa veṉiṉum mēlum āyvu tēvai. vāyppu nēriṭṭa iṭaṅkaḷil ellām oru pāl tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum maṟupāl piṟamoḻikaḷ, moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷukkum uḷḷa uṟavai veḷippaṭuttum moḻiyiyal ilakkaṇak kūṟukaḷ, coṟkaḷ, vērkaḷ ākiyavaṟṟaik kuṟippiṭṭuḷḷēṉ; kāraṇam avaikaḷukkiṭaiyilāṉa uṟavu paṟṟiya cikkalkaḷaittīrkka itu utavum eṉṟa nampikkaitāṉ.accikkalukku iṉṉum aṟiviyal pūrvamāṉa viṭai kiṭṭavillai; eṉiṉum orunāḷ kiṭṭum eṉa nampalām. ituvarai viṭaikaḷākak kūṟappaṭupavai veṟum karutukōḷkaḷē - ōraḷavu ēṟkattakkavai cilavum, ēṉaiya cilavum. intō-airōppiya moḻikkuṭumpattukkum turāṉiya allatu skitiya moḻik kuṭumpattukkum [iṉṟu urāl - altāyk eṉappaṭuvatu] naṭuvil vaikkattakkatu tirāviṭak kuṭumpam eṉpatē eṉatu muṭivu; “naṭuvil’’ eṉpatai viṭa “skitiyaṉukku caṟṟu arukil ” eṉalām. tirāviṭa moḻi kaḷukkum intō-airōppiya moḻikaḷukkum iṭaiyiluḷḷa moḻiyiyal, ilakkaṇaviyal uṟavukaḷum, col, vērccol uṟavukaḷum ērāḷamāṉavaiyum viyakkat takkavaiyumām. cila uṟupukaḷait poṟutta varaiyil avai taṟceyalāka nērntavai eṉṟu otukkiviṭa iyalavē iyalātu. eṉiṉum avvuṟavukaḷiṉ taṉmaiyaip pārkkumpoḻutu ivviru moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷukkum iṭaiyilirunta uṟavu kaḻi paḻaṅkālattaic cārntatākavumettaṉmai vāynta uṟavu eṉat tiṭṭavaṭṭamākak kūṟa iyalātatākavum uḷḷatu eṉpatē eṉatu karuttu.

tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpa moḻikaḷ intō airōppiya moḻikkuṭumpam, urāl-altāyk moḻikkuṭumpam ākiya iraṇṭu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷukkum iṭaiyē uḷḷa uṟavai viḷakkuvaṉavāka uḷḷaṉa. atu maṭṭumā? māntaṉ mutaṉmoḻi tōṉṟiya piṉṉar - am mutaṉmoḻiyiliruntu intō airōppiya moḻikkuṭumpamum , urāl-altāyk moḻik kuṭumpamum pirivataṟku muṉṉar - irunta kālattiya nilaikaḷai iṉṟum kāṭṭuvaṉavāka uḷḷaṉa eṉat tōṉṟukiṟatu. patilip peyarkaḷ (Pronouns) pōṉṟavaṟṟil tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiṉ taṉmaikaḷ kaḻipaḻaṅkāla nilaimaiyaik kāṭṭuvaṉavāka uḷḷaṉa.

tamiḻiya moḻikaḷil uḷḷa cila coṟkaḷum vērkaḷum māntaṉ mutaṉ moḻiyil iruntirukkakkūṭiyavaṭivaṅkaḷilēyē iṉṟum uḷḷaṉavākak karuta iṭamuṇṭu.

nūliṉ pakkaṅkaḷ 63-79 ( III m patippu 1913) liruntu:-skitiyaṉ moḻikaḷ maṭṭumalla, airōppā, āciyā iraṇṭu kaṇṭaṅkaḷilum uḷḷa aṉaittu moḻikaḷumē orē mūlattiliruntu tōṉṟiyavai eṉpatu elkiṉs muṭivu ākum. māntaṉ moḻi varalāṟṟaip paṟṟi namakkut terintuḷḷa ceytikaḷ perumaḷavukku ammuṭivai ātarippaṉavākavē uḷḷaṉa. āyiṉum aṟiviyal pūrvamāka immuṭivu niṟuvappaṭa iṉṉum kālam ākum. 

tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar, intiyāvil veppa maṇṭalattil uḷḷaṉar; skitiyaṉ moḻikaḷaip pēcum piṉ (Finn) makkaḷ airōppāviṉ vaṭakōṭiyilum, āstiyāk, ukriyaṉ (Ostiak, Ugrian) makkaḷ āciyāviṉ vaṭakōṭiyākiya caipīriyāvilum uḷḷaṉar. tiyūttāṉiyar(Teutons) helaṉiyar (Hellens) atāvatu kirēkkar, kelṭkaḷ Celts) ākiya intō - airōppiya moḻi pēcunar airōppāviṟkup pulam peyarntu ceṉṟu kuṭiyēṟuvataṟku muṉṉar airōppā muḻuvatum skitiyaṉ moḻi pōṉṟa (intō airōppiyamallāta) vakaimoḻi pēcunarē tol kuṭikaḷāka iruntirukka vēṇṭum. anta intō airōppiya (āriya) moḻi pēcunar pulam peyarntuvantu intiyāviṟkuḷ nuḻaivataṟku muṉṉart teṉṉintiyāviṉ tolkuṭikaḷāka tirāviṭar iruntirukka vēṇṭum. tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum intō airōppiya moḻikaḷ airōppāvil paravuvataṟku muṉṉar aṅku vaḻaṅki vanta skitiyaṉ mutaliya (intō airōppiyamallāta) moḻikaḷukkum neruṅkiya uṟavu iruppatai moḻiyiyal niṟuviyuḷḷatu evvaḷavu viyakkattakkatu! varalāṟṟuk kālattukku muṟpaṭṭa uṟavākaiyāl varalāṟṟiliruntu itaippaṟṟi oṉṟum teriya muṭiyātu. mēmpōkkākap pārttāl ittakaiya uṟavu nampamuṭiyāta putirākattāṉ tōṉṟum. “inta nilavulakam muḻuvatum paravi vāḻa aṉaittu iṉa makkaḷaiyum orē rattamāka karttār paṭaittār” eṉṉum viviliya vācakattai itu meyppikkiṟatu.

”intō airōppiya moḻikaḷiliruntu kaṭaṉ peṟappaṭātavaiyum, eṉṟālum am moḻikaḷiṉ tolpaḻaṅkālat taṉmaikaḷaiyottaṉavāka iruppaṉavum, āṉa piṉvaruvaṉavaṟṟai iṉṟumtirāviṭa moḻikaḷ koṇṭuḷḷaṉa. [atāvatu ,ikkāla moḻiyiyal naṭaiyil kūṟuvatāṉāl, tol intō-airōppiya moḻittaṉmai kaḷait tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ tāmākavē koṇṭuḷḷaṉa.]

olippu eḷimai, iṉimaikkāka ‘na’viṉ payaṉpāṭu - kirēkka moḻiyil uḷḷatu pōla
paṭarkkaiyiṭap pirati peyarkaḷilum, viṉaic coṟkaḷilum pāl vēṟupāṭu iruppatu - kuṟippāka potup pāl iruppatu.
cuṭṭup patilip peyarkaḷilum, paṭarkkaip patilip peyarkaḷilum potuppāl orumaiyaik kāṭṭa d /t payaṉpāṭu.
lattīṉil uḷḷatu pōla potuppāl paṉmaiyaik kāṭṭa a payaṉpāṭu.
cēymaic cuṭṭukku a; aṇmaic cuṭṭukku i payaṉpāṭu.
perciyaṉ moḻiyiṟpōla, perumpālum iṟanta kālattaik kāṭṭa t payaṉpāṭu
vēril oru oliyaṉai iraṭṭittu cila coṟkaḷil, iṟanta kālattaik kāṭṭutal.
viṉaic collil ōr uyireḻuttai nīṭṭi olittu viṉaiyālaṇaiyum peyarkaḷai amaittal.

“Primitive underived Indo-Europeanisms discoverable in the Dravidian Languages [in current parlance:: ‘proto-Indo European features derived from Dravidian”]

The use of n, as in Greek to prevent hiatus.
The existence of gender in the pronouns of the third person, and in verbs and in particular the existence of a neuter gender.
The use of d or t as the sign of the neuter singular of demonstrative pronouns or pronouns of the third person.
The existence of a neuter plural, as in Latin, in short a.
The formation of the remote demonstrative from a base in a; the proximate from the base in i.
The formation of most preterites, as in Persian by the addition of d
The formation of some preterites by the reduplication of a portion of the root.
The formation of a considerable number of verbal nouns by lengthening the vowel of the verbal root.

6. nallūr cuvāmi ñāṉappirakācar (1929, 1953)

“airōppiya moḻikaḷiṉ tantaiyākiya toṉ moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟattiṟkāṉa ātāraṅkaḷ tamiḻiltāṉ kiṭṭum” (Tamil supplies this long looked for clue to finding the true origina of the prot Indo European language) - ceṉṉaik kiṟittuvak kallūri itaḻ 1929

“intō airōppiya moḻikaḷil ‘vērkaḷ’ eṉa uṉṉikkappaṭupavai poruḷ toṭarpaṟṟa veṟum kuṟiyīṭukaḷtām. tirāviṭa moḻi vērkaḷōveṉil aiyattiṟkiṭamiṉṟip poruḷaiyum kāraṇattaiyum kāṭṭupavaiyāka uyirōṭṭamuḷḷa muḷaikkaru pōlat tōṉṟum; entak karuttōṭṭattil palvēṟu coṟkaḷ uruvāyiṉa eṉpatait teṟṟeṉa avai kāṭṭuvaṉavāka amaintuḷḷaṉa. ittaṉmai avaṟṟukku illātatāl intō airōppiya ‘vērkaḷ’ iruḷ maṇṭiya poruḷaṟṟa veṟum olikkuviyalkaḷākavē tōṉṟum. avaṟṟukkum teḷivu tantu oḷikāṭṭi uyirūṭṭam tarakkūṭiyavai tirāviṭa moḻi vērkaḷtām.” - Tamil Culture 1953 [piṟamoḻi vērkaḷukkum ikkaruttu perum aḷavukkup poruntuvatākum]

7. pe. cuntaram piḷḷai (1891)

“kaṉṉaṭamum kaḷi teluṅkum kaviṉ malaiyāḷamum tuḷuvum uṉ utarattu utitteḻuntē oṉṟu pala āyiṭiṉum”
- maṉōṉmaṇiyam pāyiram.

8. mākaṟal kārttikēya mutaliyār 

“tamiḻ, ulakil mutal mutal uṇṭāṉa iyaṟkai mutaṉmoḻi”

“āriyattukku muṉ tamiḻ iruntateṉpatu kaṭukkaṉukku muṉ poṉ iruntateṉpatu pōlak koḷḷal vēṇṭum.”
-moḻinūl (1913) pa. 86

maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ

“āriyar intiya nāṭṭiṟku kuṭipukutaṟku muṉ iv intiya nāṭu muḻutum paraviyirunta makkaḷ tamiḻarēyāvar”

“iṅṅaṉat tamiḻariṉ aṟivāḻa ārāycciyiṉai āriya naṉmakkaḷ cilar tāmum taḻuvi oḻukap pukunta kālattilē tāṉ upaniṭataṅkaḷ eḻutappaṭṭaṉa. iv upaniṭataṅkaḷ āriyarkku eṭṭātirunta aṟaṅkaḷai aṟivuṟutti, avar ceytu pōnta uyirkkolaiyiṉai niṟuttutaṟporuṭṭākat tamiḻc cāṉṟōrkaḷāl iyaṟṟappaṭṭaṉavā meṉpataṟku av vupaniṭataṅkaḷilēyē maṟukkappaṭāta cāṉṟukaḷ uḷḷaṉa.”
- mullaippāṭṭu ārāycciyurai (1903)

irukkuvēta kālantoṭṭē pallāyiran tamiḻc coṟkaḷ vaṭamoḻikkaṭpukuntu vaḻaṅkalāyiṉa” [vētam’ eṉṉum vaṭamoḻic col tamiḻ vērc collil uruvāṉatē eṉṟum terivittuḷḷār.] 

- tamiḻar nākarikam allatu vēḷāḷar yār? - 1923. ñā.tēvanēyap pāvāṇar

“tamiḻallāta oru moḻi tirāviṭat tāyākat tamiḻukku muṟpaṭṭu iruntatēyillai”
- tamiḻ varalāṟu ( 1967) pak.35

“tamiḻ uṇmaiyil tiraviṭattāyum āriya mūlamum ākumeṉṟum aṟintu koḷka” 
- tamiḻar varalāṟu (1972) pak. 58

“intiyāvil maṭṭumaṉṟu ulakattilēyē mutaṉ mutal neṭuṅkaṇakku vakuttatum ataṟku muṟai amaittatum tamiḻarē”

 “vaṭamoḻi vaṇṇamālai (alphabet) tamiḻ neṭuṅ kaṇakku muṟaiyai muṟṟilum taḻuviyatu.”
- vaṭamoḻi varalāṟu (1972) pak. 300

“iraṇṭāyiram āṇṭukaḷukkum atikamāṉa varalāṟu uṭaiya mikaccila ulaka moḻikaḷil tamiḻ oṉṟu. ulakiṉ pāriya moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷ oṉṟil atutāṉ, maiya iṭam vakikkiṉṟatu. tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpam cārnta, emmoḻiyai āypavarukkum toṭakkattiliruntē tamiḻ, tamiḻ varalāṟu tamiḻiṉ paṇṭai ilakkaṇam ākiyavaṟṟait teriya vēṇṭiyatu iṉṟiyamaiyātatu. tamiḻ ilakkiyac celvattai viñcum ilakkiyam ulakil vēṟu emmoḻiyilum illai. eṉavē tamiḻ ilakkiyacciṟappai ulakeṅkum aṉaivarum terintu koḷḷa vakai ceyyum moḻipeyarppup paṇikaḷ tēvai.”
- ār. ī. āṣar (2004)

“tirāviṭa” moḻik kuṭumpamā? “tamiḻiya” moḻik kuṭumpamā?

moḻiyiyalil tamiḻiya (Tamulian) moḻikkuṭumpam eṉṟē kālṭuvel kālam varai vaḻaṅkiya peyarai avar tāṉ “tirāviṭa” moḻik kuṭumpam eṉa māṟṟi vaḻaṅkiṉār. tamiḻ eṉpataṉ koccait tiripuvaṭivamē tirāviṭameṉpatākum. makāvamcattil “tamiḷa” , taṇṭi eḻutiya avanticuntari kataiyil “tramiḷa” eṉṟa collumvarukiṉṟaṉa. ikṣvāku kuṭimarapiṉariṉ pirākirutak kalveṭṭilum ‘tamiḷa’ tāṉ varukiṟatu. piṉṉar kumārilapaṭṭariṉ tantiravārttikattil

“tatyatā tirāviṭāti pāṣāyam ēva” (appaṭi tirāviṭa (=tamiḻ) maṟṟum piṟamoḻikaḷil)

eṉṉum iṭattil tamiḻ-tamiḷa -tramiḷa-tramiṭa -traviṭa - trāviṭa eṉṟu māṟiviṭṭatu. ē.ci.parṉel 1872 intiyaṉ āṉṭikuvāri mutal tokutiyil mēṟkaṇṭa tantira vārttikap pakutiyai acciṭṭapoḻutu apattamāka,  

“āntra trāviṭa pāṣāyam ēva” 

eṉṟu acciṭṭār (mēlum paṟpala piḻaikaḷuṭaṉ). intat tavaṟāṉa vācakattai kālṭuvel (1875) sṭeṉ kōṉō (Linguistic Survey of India : 1906) pōṉṟōr piṉpaṟṟiṉar. ittavaṟai pi.ṭi.cīṉivācaaiyaṅkār intiyaṉ āṉṭikuvāri 42ām tokutiyil (1913) teḷḷatteḷivāka niṟuviyuḷḷār. (kuñña&ṇṇirājā Annals of Oriental Research tokuti 28 (1979) kaṭṭuraiyil kuṟittuḷḷatu pōla parṉel uṭaiya tavaṟāṉa vācakattait tamiḻaṟiñar palarum iṉṟum piṉpaṟṟit “tamiḻaiyum, teluṅkaiyum orucērak kumārilapaṭṭar kuṟittār” eṉṟu tavaṟāka eḻutivarukiṉṟaṉar.)

“tirāviṭam” eṉṉum collil iruntu “tamiḻ” uruvāṉatu eṉṟu kālṭuvel tavaṟākak kuṟippiṭṭatu paṟṟi avaraik kuṟai kūṟal ollātu. akkālattil tamiḻ cārnta moḻiyiyal āyvukaḷ irunta nilai avvaḷavutāṉ. āṉāl avarukkup piṉṉar 1887il kalittokaip patippuraiyil ci.vai.tāmōtaraṉār toṭaṅki, kamil culepil (Journal of the Institute of Asian Madras IV -2 :1987) muṭiya nūṟṟukkaṇakkāṉa moḻiyiyalaṟiñar aṉaivarum “tamiḻ” eṉṉum colliṉ tiripuṟṟa vaṭivamē “tirāviṭam” eṉṟu āṇittaramāka niṟuviyuḷḷa nilaiyil iṉṟum “tirāviṭam” eṉṉum colliliruntu “tamiḻ” eṉṉum col uruvākiyatu eṉa kūṟikkoṇṭiruppatu aṟiyāmaiyē ākum.

tirāviṭam (Proto - Dravidian) eṉṟu vaṇṇaṉai moḻi nūlāyvāḷar mīṭṭuruvākkam ceyvaṉavaṟṟuḷ pala piḻaiyāy muṭikiṉṟaṉa eṉpataiyum, paḻantamiḻē itaṟkuk kaṭṭaḷaik kallākak koḷḷappaṭa vēṇṭum eṉpataiyum pāvāṇar niṟuvi viṭṭār. tamiḻiṉ tiripukaḷē piṟa tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ. paḻantamiḻiṉiṉṟum vēṟupaṭṭatāka tol tirāviṭam eṉpatu kaṟpaṉaiyē eṉpar pāvāṇar. ākavē tirāviṭam, tol tirāviṭam eṉa kuṟippiṭappaṭuvaṉa vellām uṇmaiyil paḻantamiḻaiyē kuṟippatāka uṇarka. eṉiṉum ulaka aḷavil “tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpam” eṉpatu oru kuṟiyīṭṭuc col ākiviṭṭa nilaiyil atai nām payaṉpaṭuttuvatil tavaṟillai. ataṉ uṇmaip poruḷ ‘tamiḻiya moḻik kuṭumpam’ eṉpatē.

iyal – 2

tamiḻiṉ taṉittaṉmai toṉmai, muṉmai,
teṉmai, ciṟappu, ñālamutaṉ moḻikku ataṉ
nerukkam.

(kālṭuvel oppilakkaṇa nūlil iruntu eṭutta pakutikaḷ - iyal 1il kaṇṭavai- ivviyaliṉ poruṇmaiyum cārntavaivēyām. avarukkup piṉ vanta āyvukaḷum kuṟippākaaṇmaikkāla āyvukaḷum ipporuṇmaiyai valuvāka ātarippatai ivviyalil uḷḷa karuttut tokuppukaḷiliruntu aṟika.)

1. hec.ār.hāl (1913)

“cumēriya uruvaccilaikaḷ, puṭaippuc ciṟpaṅkaḷaip pārkkum poḻutu paṇṭaic cumēriyar uṭalamaipput tōṟṟam avarkaḷaic cūḻa irunta cemittiyar, āriyar pōṉṟavarkaḷiṭamiruntu māṟupaṭṭu iruntatu - cemittiya, āriya moḻikaḷiliruntu cumēriya moḻiyum māṟupaṭṭu iruntatu pōla. cumēriyar uṭalamaipput tōṟṟam intiyar pōṉṟē iruntatu. iṉṟum carācariintiyaṉuṭaiya tōṟṟam āyirakkaṇakkāṉa āṇṭukaḷukku muntaiya varalāṟuṭaiya avaṉuṭaiya tirāviṭa muṉṉōruṭaiya tōṟṟam tāṉ; (intiyāviṟkuḷ vantēṟiya) āriyar uṭalamaipput tōṟṟam eppoḻutō maṟaintu viṭṭatu. (itu pōlavē airōppāvilum kirīs, ittāliya nāṭṭu makkaḷiṉ potuvāṉa uṭalamaipput tōṟṟam annāṭukaḷil kuṭiyēṟiya āriyarkaḷuṭaiyataip pōṉṟu illāmal, avarkaḷukku muntaiya - airōppiya āriyarallāta - paḻaṅkuṭikaḷiṉ tōṟṟam ākavē uḷḷatu.) ākavē teṉ intiyāvil iṉṟu vacittuk koṇṭu tirāviṭa moḻi pēci varum, tirāviṭaṉ uṭalamaipput tōṟṟam tāṉ cumēriyaṉ tōṟṟamum. intiyāviliruntu tarai vaḻiyākavum [kaṭal vaḻiyākavum?] pāracīkam vaḻiyāka mecopotāmiyā ceṉṟu kuṭiyēṟiya (tirāviṭa) iṉattavarē cumēriyar eṉak karuta iṭamuḷḷatu...iṉṟum palūcistāṉattil tirāviṭa moḻiyāṉa pirāhuy pēcum makkaḷ uḷḷaṉar; pāracīkat teṉ pakutiyil tirāviṭar pōṉṟa uṭalamaippuḷḷa makkaḷ vacikkiṉṟaṉar. pāracīkattil paṇṭu vāḻntatāka kirēkkar kūṟum “aṉāriyakō” (Anariakoi) makkaḷum (intiyāviṟkum pāpilōṉukkum iṭaippaṭṭa pakutiyil paṇṭu taṅkiviṭṭa) tirāviṭarkaḷāka irukkalām. paṇṭaicumēriya nakaraṅkaḷāṉa eritu pōṉṟavaṟṟukku mutaṉmutalil nākarikattai kiḻakkiliruntu pāracīka vaḷaikuṭā vaḻiyāka vanta oyāṉas eṉṉum mīṉ - maṉitaṉ koṇṭu vantatāka (ki.mu. 5ām nū.cārnta) perōcas kataiyiṉ uṭkaruttu eṉṉa? cumēriyāvukku kaṭal vaḻiyākavum (tirāviṭa) nākarikamceṉṟirukkalām eṉpatē atu. intiyāvil tāṉ (cintuveḷiyil?) tirāviṭanākarikam mutalil vaḷarntirukka vēṇṭum. aṅku tāṉ eḻuttukku varivaṭivam (Script) mutaṉ mutalil kaṇṭupiṭikkappaṭṭu, mutalil paṭaveḻuttāka iruntu piṉṉar tiruntiya eḷiya vari vaṭivam uruvākkappaṭṭu, cumēriyāvukkum paravi aṅku nāḷaṭaivil kuṉēpārm āppeḻuttu uruppeṟṟirukka vēṇṭum. cumēriyā cellum vaḻiyil tirāviṭar elām pakutiyilum taṅkaḷ nākarikattai viṭṭuc ceṉṟirukka vēṇṭum. eṉavē cumēriyāvukku nākarikattaip parappiyatākak kūṟappaṭum (cemittiyarum allāta, āriyarum allāta) putiyavarkaḷ intiyāviliruntu aṅku ceṉṟa tirāviṭarāka irukkalām. āṉāl itaṟku nēraṭi ātāram illai. eṉavē itai oru muṟṟāṉa muṭivāka alla; oru karutukōḷāka nāṉ muṉ vaikkiṟēṉ.”

-H.R. Hall (1913) Ancient History of the Near East mecapotāmiyāvil kuṭiyēṟiya tamiḻarē cumēriya nākarikattai uruvākkiyirukkalām eṉṉum ikkaruttai pi.ṭi.cīṉivāca aiyaṅkār tamatu History of the Tamils (1929) nūlil ēṟṟuḷḷār. kāntiratāv (The Riddles of the Three Oceans; (1974) tamiḻākkam “tamiḻa nākarikamum cintuveḷi nākarikamum: teṉ peruṅkaṭal āyvukaḷ(2007)) avarkaḷukkum ikkaruttu ēṟpuṭaiyatē.


2. es.heṉṟi hīrās

speyiṉ nāṭṭaic cārnta tirāviṭar eṉṟu taṉṉai aḻaittuk koṇṭa es.hīrās pātiriyār (11.09.1888 - 14.12.1955) toṉmai intō -naṇṇilakkarai nākarika āyvu (Studies in Proto-Indo Mediterranean Culture 1953) eṉṉum nūlil cintuveḷi nākarikam tirāviṭaruṭaiyatu; avarkaḷ moḻi tirāviṭa moḻi eṉpatai niṟuviyuḷḷār. mikap paḻaṅkālattil (ki.mu.5000kkum neṭuṅkālattukku muṉṉar) mutaṟkaṇ kāvirikkaraiyil uruvākiya arappā nākarikam intiya mēṟkuk karai vaḻiyākat tamiḻarkaḷāl cintuveḷi, cumēriyā, ekiptu, naṇṇilakkarai nāṭukaḷ varai koṇṭu cellappaṭṭatu eṉpatu hīrās koḷkai. [avar vātattukku ātāramākak koṇṭa ceytikaḷ cila ippoḻutu tavaṟākat tōṉṟalām; cintuveḷi muttiraikaḷai avar tirāviṭa moḻi cārntavai eṉṟu coṉṉa muṭivu cariyeṉṟālum avar muttiraikaḷil paṭittuk kaṇṭa vācakaṅkaḷ iṉṟu ēṟkattakkaṉavāka illai. iruntālum avar kaṇṭa aṭippaṭai uṇmai iṉṟum valuvuṭaiya tākavē uḷḷatu]

3. eṉ.lakōvāri

i) 6000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar intiyāviliruntu aṭlāṇṭik kaṭalvarai orē mātiriyāṉa “pala col piṇippu oṭṭunilai” (Polysynthetic suffixal) moḻikaḷ, iṭaiyīṭu iṉṟip paraviyiruntaṉa. avaṟṟiṉ coṟkaḷañciyaṅkaḷ ōraḷavukku oṉṟupōl iruntaṉa.potuccol viḻukkāṭu moḻikku moḻi māṟupaṭṭu iruntirukkalām. tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ, elām, cumēriyaṉ, harri, kākēciyaṉ, halti, pōṉṟavai orē moḻiyamaippuk koṇṭu, oṉṟukkoṉṟu uṟavuṭaiyavaiyākac caṅkilit toṭar pōla amaintiruntaṉa.

ii) intō airōppiya moḻi pēcunar (ki.mu.2000ai oṭṭi) airōppāvukkuḷ kiḻakkiliruntu nuḻaiyumuṉṉar aṅku pēcappaṭṭu vanta moḻikaḷil oṉṟāṉa pāsku (yūskarā) iṉṟum pirāṉcukkum speyiṉukkum iṭaippaṭṭa pireṉīs malaippakutiyil pēcappaṭṭu varukiṟatu. tol tirāviṭa moḻi inta pāsku moḻiyuṭaṉ maṭṭumiṉṟi intō airōppiya moḻikaḷukku muṉṉar airōppāvil pēcappaṭṭa toṉ moḻikaḷuṭaṉum uṟavuṭaiyatu.

iii) immoḻikaḷ aṉaittum oṉṟukkoṉṟu neruṅkiya toṭarpuḷḷavai; orē potuvāṉa tāy moḻiyiṉ vaṭṭāra vaḻakkukaḷ eṉṟē kūṟalām.

- N. Lahovary (1963) tirāviṭar tōṟṟamum mēl nāṭukaḷum (Dravidian origins and the west) “tirāviṭar naṇṇilakkarai nāṭukaḷiliruntu pulam peyarntu vantu intiyāvil cintuveḷiyil mutaṟkaṇ kuṭiyēṟip piṉṉar kumari varaip paraviṉar” eṉṟa apattak koḷkaiyai avar (vivaram puriyāmal) piṉpaṟṟiyirunta pōtilum innūl ciṟanta moḻiyiyal meymmaikaḷai uṇarttuvatākum.

4. piriṭjaṭ & rēmaṇṭ ālciṉ (1988)

“teṉṉintiyā iṉṟu tirāviṭamoḻikaḷ vaḻaṅkum pakuti. ammoḻikaḷaip pēcuvōr pūrvīkam eṉṉavāka iruntirukkalām? avarkaḷ (vaṭamēṟkiliruntu) teṉṉintiyāviṟku vantu kuṭiyēṟumpōtu tammuṭaṉ irumpaiyum peruṅ kaṟpaṭai nākarikattaiyum koṇarntaṉar eṉa (valuvāṉa ātāra miṉṟik) kūṟappaṭukiṟatu. tolliyal cāṉṟukaḷiṉpaṭi ippaṭi naṭantirukka vāyppu mikak kuṟaivu. irumpup payaṉ pāṭṭiṟku 1000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar iruntē karunāṭakattil (teṉ intiyāvil piṟa pakutikaḷilum kūṭa irukkalām) peruṅkaṟpaṭai nākarikak kuṭiyiruppukaḷ iruntuḷḷaṉa;avai iṭaiyīṭu iṉṟit toṭarntu iruntu vantuḷḷaṉa; viyakkat takka paṇpāṭṭut toṭarcciyuṭaṉ iṉṟuma uḷḷa paṇpāṭṭuk kūṟukaḷ pala antap paḻaiya nākarikak kūṟukaḷiṉ toṭarcciyākavē tōṉṟukiṉṟaṉa. uṭaṟkūṟu amaippilum antap paḻaiya nākarika makkaḷum iṉṟaiya makkaḷum perum aḷavukku oṉṟu pōlavē uḷḷaṉar. eṉavē teṉṉintiyāvil (pallāyiram āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar iruntē) putukkaṟkāla nākarikam paṭaitta makkaḷiṭaiyē uruvāṉavaiyē tirāviṭamoḻikaḷ eṉpatai maṟuppatu kaṭiṉamāka uḷḷatu. teṉṉintiyāvil paṟpala pakutikaḷil irunta putukkaṟkāla nākarika makkaḷiṭaiyē toṭarpu iruntirukkum; potuvāṉa tirāviṭa moḻiyum iruntirukkum. ki.mu.3000-1000 kāla aḷavil teṉṉintiyāvil irunta putuk kaṟkāla kuṭiyiruppukaḷil iruntu piṟantavaiyē varalāṟṟuk kālat toṭakkattaic cārnta kuṭiyiruppukaḷum eṉalām.

The southern part of the peninsula is today the homeland of the Dravidian Language, and we may well enquire what- speaking in broadest terms-is likely to have been their history. It has been claimed, though not on very solid grounds, that the earliest speakers of these languages brought with them into Peninsular India both Iron and the custom of making megalithic graves. In the light of archaeological evidence this appears to be extremely improbale. We now know that atleast for a millennium prior to the arrival of iron, there were established settlements in Karnataka, and probably also in other parts of the peninsula, and these settlements show evidence of a remarkable continuity of culture. Many modern culture traits appear to derive from them, and a substantial part of the populations shows physical affilation to the Neolithic people. In the Light of all this it is difficult to believe that the Dravidian languages do not owe their origin to the same people who produced the Neolithic culture there. xxxx the several regional cultures.... had throughout a degree of interaction and probably originally a common language family, Dravidian. xxxx the settlements of the third - second millennia appear to be ancestral to those which we encounter there from the beginnings of history
onwards (p.353) 

- Bridget and Raymond Allchin (1988) The rise of Civilisation in India and Pakistan

rikvētam tokukkappaṭṭa kālam ki.mu. 1500 - 1300ākum. intō āriya moḻip paṭaippukaḷil mikappaḻaiyatu atuvē. avvētattilēyē intō- āriyamallātaṉavum, tirāviṭat taṉmai vāyntaṉavumāṉa pala kūṟukaḷ – oliyaṉ māṟṟaṅkaḷ, tirāviṭattiliruntu kaṭaṉpeṟṟa coṟkaḷ, iṭappeyarkaḷ, makkaṭ peyarkaḷ ākiyavai uḷḷaṉa. ki.mu.1500kku muṉṉar cila nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷ kālam vaṭamēṟku intiyāvil vētamoḻi pēciyavarkaḷum tirāviṭa moḻi pēciyavarkaḷum neruṅkiya paṇpāṭṭut toṭarpu koṇṭu vāḻntirukka vēṇṭum eṉpatai itu kāṭṭukiṟatu.
- mēlatu

The earliest Indo - Aryan text, the compiled Rg Veda, shows several influences of a non -Indo - Aryan, Dravidian element in the form of phonetic changes. introduction of loan words and names etc. These presuppose the coexistence of the Vedic and Dravidian speaking peoples in a cultural contact situtation for a period, perhaps of centuries, before the compilation of the Rg Veda (circa 1500 - 1300 BC)

[aṭuttu varum pakkaṅkaḷil “puṭaviyiṉ tōṟṟatti liruntu iṉṟaiya māntaṉ varai” eṉṉum viḷakkap paṭam tarappaṭṭuḷḷatu. mānta iṉat tōṟṟam , moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟam pōṉṟavai kuṟittu palvēṟu aṟiviyaṟ pulaṅkaḷil iṉṟaiya aṟiñarkaḷ ēṟkum muṭivukaḷai atu kāṭṭukiṟatu. ]

5. ñāla mutaṉmoḻi kuṟittum ataṟkut tamiḻ mika neruṅkiya teṉpatu kuṟittum aṇmaikkālattil uruvākiyuḷḷa karuttōṭṭaṅkaḷ:

(1) iṉṟu ulakil pēcappaṭum 6000 moḻikaḷum palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷaic cērntavaiyāka vakaip paṭuttappaṭṭaḷḷaṉa. akkuṭumpaṅkaḷuḷ mukkiyamāṉavai intō-airōppiyam, tirāviṭam, urāl-altāyk, āprō āciyaṉ (hamaitō - cemittik) ākiyavaiyākum. innāṉku moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷumē potuvāṉa oru moḻip peruṅkuṭumpattiliruntu Macrofamily tōṉṟiyirukka vēṇṭum eṉṟu 1900 ai oṭṭi airōppiya moḻiyilaṟiñar cilar karutiṉar. ammoḻip peruṅ kuṭumpattukku 1904-il ṭeṉmārk aṟiñar hālter petarcaṉ nāstirātik eṉṟu peyar cūṭṭiṉār. lattīṉ moḻiyil nāstirās eṉṟāl nammavaṉ eṉṟu poruḷ. accolliliruntu nāstirātik (namatumoḻi) eṉṟa collai petarcaṉ uruvākkiṉār; ivvāyvukaḷaik kuṟittiṭa nāstirātiks eṉṉum col uruvākkap paṭṭuḷḷatu. 1960kaḷil raciya moḻiyiyal aṟiñar ilic cuvitic ittuṟaiyil virivāṉa āyvukaḷ ceytār. antō! avar 1965il untu vipattil iṟantu viṭṭār. nāstirātik tavira ēṉaiya moḻip peruṅkuṭumpaṅkaḷāka moḻiyiyalaṟiñar iṉṟu ēṟṟuḷḷa ēṉaiya moḻip peruṅkuṭumpaṅkaḷ varumāṟu: cīṉa - kākēciyaṉ, āstirik, amerikkai ntiyaṉ, intō-pacipik, kōycāṉ, kāṅkō-cakārā

2. petarcaṉ, cuvitic ākiyōr niṟuviya nāstirātik, amerikkar kirīṉperku 2000il niṟuviya yūrēciyāṭik ivviraṇṭukkum iṭaiyē atika vēṟupāṭu illai. kāṇka:

1. nāstirātik moḻip peruṅkuṭumpattil aṭaṅkiya moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷ:
1965il cuvitic karuttuppaṭi:

intō-airōppiya moḻikaḷ, urālik (piṉṉiṣ mutaliyavai), altāyik (turukkiyam mutaliyavai), tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ, kārṭveliyaṉ (jārjiya moḻi mutaliyavai) āprō-āciyaṉ (cemittiya moḻikaḷum hāmittiya moḻikaḷum)

2. piṟṟai nāstirātik āyvāḷar karuttuppaṭi:

āprō-āciya moḻikaḷai nīkkuka:- stārōstiṉ, cevarōskiṉ, alekcis māṉāsṭar rēmar
cukōtiyaṉ, eskimō-ālyūt cērkka : tālkōpālski;
cappāṉiyam, koriyam cērkka (altāyik kuṭumpattil allatu taṉiyāka) pāmhārṭu, stārōskiṉ
kārṭveliyaṉai nīkkuka:- pāmhārṭu, kerṉs
elāmaiṭṭai cērkka:- tālkōpālski
cumēriyattaic cērkka:- pāmhārṭu

(2) kirīṉ perk-iṉ yūrēciyāṭik moḻip peruṅkuṭumpattil aṭaṅkiyavai;-

etraskaṉ, intō-airōppiya moḻikaḷ, urālik - yūkākir, altāyik, koriyaṉ-jappāṉiyam-jaṉu, cukōtiyaṉ, eskimō-alyūt.

3. rōjar piḷeñc & mātyū spiriks patippitta tolliyalum moḻiyum (1997/1999 rauṭleṭj) nūl nāṉku maṭalaṅkaḷ koṇṭatu. nāstirātik moḻip peruṅkuṭumpattil tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiṉ iṭam kuṟittu iraṇṭu vēṟupaṭṭa karuttukkaḷai annūlil kāṇalām:-

4. sṭāṉpōrṭu palkalaikkaḻakattil 2000 akṭōpar mātam patrirāju kiruṣṇamūrttiyiṭam pēcumpoḻutu kirīṉperku “tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpam yūrēciyāṭikkiṉ uṭaṉpiṟappāka (cakōtariyāka) irukka muṭiyumēyoḻiya makaḷāka irukka muṭiyātu” eṉṟār. (kiruṣṇamūrtti The Dravidian Languages 2003 pak.46) itaṉ poruḷ eṉṉa? intō airōppiya moḻik kuṭumpattil piṟanta uṭkuṭumpaṅkaḷē slāv, irāṉiyaṉ; vētamoḻi; camaṟkirutam, kirīk, lattīṉ, kelṭik, cerumāṉiyam mutaliyavai. tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ ivaṟṟukku maṭṭumalla, ivaṟṟiṉ tāyāṉa, intō-airōppiya moḻikkuṭumpattukkum muntaiya paḻamai vāyntatu eṉpatē kirīṉperk karuttu.
5. nāstirātik toṉmoḻikkum muntiyatākiya ñālamutaṉmoḻi (Mother Tongue of man allatu Proto- World) paṟṟi iṉṟu moḻiyiyalaṟiñar kōṭpāṭu eṉṉa? aṭlāṉṭik maṉtli eṉṉum amerikka itaḻil (1991 ēpral: pak.39-68) rāparṭ raiṭ eṉpavar itupaṟṟi nayampaṭa eḻutiyuḷḷār. attakaiya ñāla mutaṉmoḻi cārnta āyvukaḷil iṉṟu īṭupaṭṭuḷḷavarkaḷ meriṭ rūlaṉ, jāṉ peṅṭcaṉ, vāklāv pilācak, vitāli cevarōṣkiṉ (Merrit - Ruhlen, John Bengtson, Vaclav Blazek, Vitaly Shevoroshkin) pōṉṟavarkaḷāvar. yūrēciyāṭik paṟṟi kirīṉperk iṟappataṟku muṉ kaṭaiciyāka eḻutiya nūl intō-airōppiya moḻikaḷum avaṟṟōṭu neruṅkiya uṟavuṭaiyaṉavum - yūrēciyāṭik moḻip peruṅ kuṭumpam. Indo-European and its closest relaties: the Eurasiatic Language Family: Vol 1; Grammar; Vol II: Lexicon” (sṭāṉ pōrṭu palkalaikkaḻaka accakam, 2000/2002) eṉpatākum.

6. palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷukkiṭaiyēyuḷḷa uṟavu (atāvatu tol paḻaṅkālattila avai evvāṟu oṉṟāka iruntu paṭippaṭiyākap pirintu oṉṟu – palavāki yirukkalām eṉpatu) paṟṟi eḻutum moḻiyiyalāḷarkaḷiṭaiyē ār.el.tirāsk (R.L.TraskHistorical Linguistics; Arnold, London 1996) uṭaiya piṉvarum karuttē viruppu veṟuppaṟṟatum ciṟantatum ākum

“tol-nāstirātik cumār 15000-10000 āṇṭu kaḷukku muṉṉar pēcappaṭṭirukkalām, moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷiṉ kālvaḻi paṟṟiya karutukōḷkaḷilēyē nāstirātik tāṉ perumaḷavu ēṟkat takkatāyuḷḷatu. iṉṉum pattāṇṭukaḷil (tirāsk itai eḻutiyatu 1996il)nāstirātik koḷkai palarālum ēṟkappaṭalām”

(tirāviṭa, urāl-altāyk, cappāṉiya moḻikaḷ 10000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉarp pirintirukkalām eṉpar cuvelapil (1990) (Dravidian Linguistics - an Introduction.)

7. iṉṟu ulakil uḷḷa 660 kōṭi makkaḷumē hōmō cēpiyaṉs allatu “taṟkāla uṭalamaippu māntar” (Anatomically Modern Humans) eṉṉum orē iṉattaic cērntavarkaḷ eṉpatum avviṉam cumār oṉṟarai ilaṭcam āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar āppirikkāvil tōṉṟiyatu eṉpatum, moḻi eṉpatu 50,000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉart tōṉṟiyirukkalām eṉpatum palvēṟu aṟiviyal tuṟai viññāṉikaḷiṉ iṉṟaiya orumitta muṭivākum. mutal tāymoḻi tōṉṟiya ēṟattāḻa aimpatāyiram āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar iruntutāṉ, taṟkāla mānta iṉam viraintu ulakiṉ aṉaittuk kaṇṭaṅkaḷukkum paravuvataṟkāṉa cūḻnilai ēṟpaṭṭirukkalām eṉpatum, ap paraval teṉ intiyā vaḻiyāka naṭaipeṟṟirukkalām eṉpatum avarkaḷ karuttākum. tol tamiḻ (tirāviṭa) tōṟṟamāṉatu moḻi uruvākiyatum taṟkāla mānta iṉam ulakeṅkum paravat toṭaṅkiyatumāṉa aimpatāyiram āṇṭut toṉmai uṭaiyatākalām.( Christiansen M.H.et.al (2003) Language Evolution:Colin Masica (2001) etc..

intumākkaṭalil intiyāvukkut teṟkilirunta teṉṉāṭṭup pakutikaḷ paṇṭu kaṭalkoḷḷappaṭṭut tamiḻakap parappu curuṅkiyatākap paḻantamiḻ ilakkiyaṅkaḷum marapuc ceytikaḷum kūṟuvatu ēṟattāḻa ki.mu.8000ai oṭṭiyum piṉṉarum ulakeṅkum kaṇṭattiṭṭup pakutikaḷ (Continential shelf) kaṭaluḷ mūḻkiyataiyē kuṟippiṭuvatākak koḷvatu maṭṭumē iṉṟaiya aṟiviyalukku ottatu eṉpatai irāmanātaṉ Dravidian studies (tirāviṭap palkalaikkaḻakam, kuppam) 2003 kaṭṭuraiyil kāṇka. aṭuttuḷḷa paṭam mānta iṉapparavalilum moḻiyiṉ paravalilum teṉṉintiyāvum, tamiḻakamum iṉṟaikku 50,000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar vakitta mukkiya paṅkaik kāṭṭuvatākum:-

8. aṇmaiyil moḻiyiyalaṟiñar stepāṉ hilyar leviṭ tamatu 2007 kaṭṭuraiyil mēṟkaṇṭa kōṭpāṭaṭai ātarikkiṟār.

ammakkaḷukkum tirāviṭarukkum uḷḷa toṭarpaik kāṭṭukiṉṟaṉa. ki.mu.6000ai oṭṭi ulakeṅkum kaṭal maṭṭam uyarntu, nilappakutikaḷiṉ parappu curuṅkiya poḻutu ulakiṉ piṟa pakuti makkaḷuṭaiya nākarikaṅkaḷiṭam iruntu tuṇṭikkappaṭṭaṉar āstirēliyap paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷ. avarkaḷ teṉṉintiyāviliruntu āstirēliyāvukkup pulam peyarntatu ēṟattāḻa 40,000āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar irukkalām eṉkiṉṟaṉar āstirēliya aṟiñar.itiliruntu kuṟaintatu 40,000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉarē tirāviṭar intiyāvil iruntaṉar eṉpatu teḷivākiṉṟatu. inta [“tirāviṭar ēṟṟam”] kōṭpāṭṭiṉpaṭi tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar teṉṉintiyāviliruntu vaṭanāṭu celkiṉṟaṉar; piṉṉar aṅkiruntu pāracīkattiṟkum atait tāṇṭip piṟa nilappakutikaḷukkum celkiṉṟaṉar. anta piṟa nilappakutikaḷil mūla tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpattiliruntu pirintu urālik, altāyik, intō-airōppiyam ākiya moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷ uruvākiṉṟaṉa. intō-airōppiya moḻik kuṭumpattukku tirāviṭam tāyā? tamakkaiyā? eṉpatai iṉṟaiya nilaiyil tiṭṭa vaṭṭamākak kūṟa iyalātu”

1. stepāṉ hilyar leviṭ: “intō airōppiya moḻikaḷukkum tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum iṭaiyēyuḷḷa toṭarpukaḷ” (intō airōppiya moḻiyiyal āyvitaḻ) maṭalam 28: eṇ.3,4 cūṉ ṭica.2000 pak.407-438)pāvāṇar tamatu “The Primary classical language of the world(1966)” nūlil tamiḻ moḻiyiliruntu intō airōppiya moḻikaḷiṟ pukuntuḷḷaṉavākak kuṟippiṭuvaṉavaṟṟuḷ nūṟṟukku mēṟpaṭṭavai ēṟkattakkaṉaveṉpatu ivar karuttu.

2) tamatu (2000) nūlil kirīṉperk yūrēciyāṭik peruṅkuṭumpattiṉ mukkiyamāṉa ilakkaṇa oppumaikaḷ 72-aik kuṟippiṭṭuḷḷār. anta 72 iṉaṅkaḷil 20-kku mēṟpaṭṭavaṟṟukkut tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiṉ ilakkaṇak kūṟukaḷum porunti varukiṉṟaṉa.

3) hec.pi.ē.hakōlā (piṉlāntu) (i) āyiram turāljaṉ (DURALJAN) vērccoṟkaḷ (2000); innūlil tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ, urāl-altāyk iṉa moḻikaḷ, cappāṉiya moḻi, teṉ amerikka perunāṭṭu koṣuvā moḻi, ākiyavaṟṟiṭaiyē 1000 aṭippaṭai oppumaic coṟkaḷait tantuḷḷār.

4) cumēriya moḻiyum tolturāljaṉ moḻikaḷum (2003): hajat acātiyaṉ uṭaṉ iṇaintu eḻutiyatu. muntaiya nūliṟ kaṇṭa 1000 oppumaic coṟkaḷil 410 coṟkaḷ (ki.mu.3500-1800 kālakkaṭṭattaic cārnta) cumēriya moḻic coṟkaḷōṭum ottiruppatai niṟuvukiṟatu:

10) tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum piṟa moḻikaḷukkum iṭaiyēyuḷḷa uṟavukaḷ kuṟittu ituvarai nikaḻntuḷḷa āyvukaḷiṉ paṭṭiyal varumāṟu:-

11) ñālamutaṉmoḻi āyvāḷar meriṭ rūlaṉ taṉatu moḻikaḷiṉ tōṟṟam - moḻikaḷiṉ koṭi vaḻi āyvu sṭāṉpōrṭu palkalaikkaḻakam, 1994 nūliṉ pakkam 277 il kūṟuvatu varumāṟu:

 “perumpālāṉa moḻiyiyalaṟiñarkaḷ ottuk koḷḷāviṭiṉum allatu aiyappāṭṭuṭaṉ karutiṉum iṉṟuḷḷa moḻikaḷ aṉaittum (iṉṟu iṟantuviṭṭa āṉāl muṉṉarnilaviyataṟkuc cāṉṟukaḷ kiṭṭiyuḷḷa moḻikaḷ uṭpaṭa) orē ñāla mutaṉ moḻiyiliruntu tāṉ tōṉṟiṉa eṉpataṟku valuvāṉa ātāram uḷḷatu.”

annūliṉ pakkaṅkaḷ 277-366il 27 mukkiyamāṉa karuttu kaḷukku palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷilum uḷḷa coṟkaḷ “Global Etymologies” tarappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. akkaruttukaḷukku ñāla mutaṉmoḻiyil eṉṉa vērccol iruntirukkalām eṉṟu ūkikka atu muṉṉōṭiyākum. avaṟṟuḷ palavaṟṟukku (ēṟattāḻa 17kku) t tamiḻc coṟkaḷē poruḷ potintaṉavākavum ñālamutaṉmoḻiyiṉ vērc col vaṭivai viḷakkuvaṉavākavum amaintuḷḷaṉa; “tamiḻē iyaṉ moḻi’ eṉap pāvāṇar ceppiyatai itu meyppikkiṟatu)

“perumpālāṉa moḻiyiyalaṟiñarkaḷ ottuk koḷḷā viṭiṉum allatu aiyappāṭṭuṭaṉ karutiṉum iṉṟuḷḷa moḻikaḷ aṉaittum (iṉṟu iṟantuviṭṭa āṉāl muṉṉar nilaviyataṟkuc cāṉṟukaḷ kiṭṭiyuḷḷa moḻikaḷ uṭpaṭa) orē ñāla mutaṉmoḻiyiliruntu tāṉ tōṉṟiṉa eṉpataṟku valuvāṉa ātāram uḷḷatu”.

13. centamiḻc celvi oṉpatām maṭalam (1931-32) 308m pakkattil pāvāṇar ‘vaṭamoḻic ceṉṟa teṉ coṟkaḷ’ kaṭṭuraiyil “ettaṉaiyō teṉcoṟkaḷ vaṭamoḻic ceṉṟu vākukarāy vaḻaṅki varukiṉṟaṉa. avai naḷarākum kālamum naṇṇum” eṉṟār. akkaṭṭurai vaṭamoḻi paṟṟiyatu maṭṭumē yātaliṉ avvāṟu kūṟiṉār. piṉṉar aṭutta 50 āṇṭukaḷil intō, airōppiya moḻikaḷ, piṟamoḻik
kuṭumpam cār moḻikaḷ ākiyavaṟṟilum mikappala tamiḻc coṟkaḷ ēṟiyuḷḷataip pāvāṇarē niṟuviyuḷḷārākaliṉ,  “vaṭamoḻic ceṉṟu” eṉpatai “piṟamoḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷiṭaic ceṉṟu” eṉak koḷvatu poruttam. ñālattiṉ palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷiṭaic ceṉṟu vākukarāy vaḻaṅkum tamiḻc coṟkaḷ naḷarākum kālam. (atāvatu pāvāṇariṉ kaṉavu naṉavākum kālam) naṇṇiviṭṭatu eṉalām.

14. aṇmaiyil cemmoḻi ak-tica2007 itaḻil stepāṉ leviṭ ñālamutaṉmoḻi cār āyvukaḷil tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiṉ mukkiyattuvattaip piṉvarumāṟu valiyuṟuttiyuḷḷār:-

nāstirātik āyvukaḷai 1960-70 kaḷil toṭaṅkip perum aḷavil vaḷarac ceytavarkaḷ (raciyar) vilātislāv illic - cuvitic, intiyāvil tēvanēyaṉ ākiyōrāvar. palkalaikkaḻaka nilai moḻiyiyal tuṟaikaḷil kōlōcciyavarkaḷ, “nāstirātik” āyvukaḷait tīṇṭattakātaṉa vākak karutiya kālam atu. illic-cuvitic, tēvanēyaṉ iruvarum ittuṟaiyil uṇmai oḷi kāṇa vaḻi kāṭṭiya muṉṉōṭikaḷ. palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷai iṇaittu, “moḻip peruṅkuṭumpaṅkaḷ Superfamilies āka vakaip paṭuttic ceyyum āyvukaḷaip palkalaikkaḻaka nilai moḻiyiyalaṟiñarum ēṟkum nilai iṉṟu uruvākiyuḷḷatu. attakaiya āyvukaḷil mutaliṭam peṟa vēṇṭiya tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiṉ mukkiyattuvam iṉṉum uṇarappaṭavillai.. tamiḻc cērccoṟkaḷiṉ aṭippaṭaiyil tēvanēyaṉ muṉ vaittuḷḷa moḻiyiyal vātaṅkaḷ palavum nāstirātik pōṉṟa āyvukaḷil tirāviṭamoḻikaḷ vakikkum mukkiya iṭattai viḷakkukiṉṟaṉa. attakaiya āyvukaḷil ‘tirāviṭa intō airōppiya” col uṟavu āyvum oṉṟu; avaṟṟuḷ mukkiyamāṉatum kūṭa” 
moḻiyiyal cārnta aṟiñar cilar karuttukaḷ (ivviyalōṭu toṭarpuṭaiyavai)

6. “intiya oru moḻiyiyaṟpulam” āyvāḷarkaḷ tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiruntu intō āriya moḻikaḷukkuc ceṉṟuḷḷaṉavākak karutum ilakkaṇak kūṟukaḷum, moḻiyiyal kūṟukaḷum

intiya moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷiṭaiyē potumai kuṟittu mutaṉ mutalil virivāka 1956 il (Language, 32; pak 2-16) “intiyā oru moḻiyiyaṟ pulam India as a Linguistic Area  eṉṉum kaṭṭuraiyil eḻutiyavar emeṉō intiyāvil uḷḷa tamiḻiya, intō āriya, muṇṭā iṉa moḻikaḷiṭaiyē potumaik kūṟukaḷ (perum pālāṉavai tamiḻiya moḻikaḷiṭamiruntu piṟavaṟṟukkup paraviyavai) uḷḷatai akkaṭṭurai niṟuviyatu. ikkōṭ pāṭṭiṉ karu veku muṉṉarē uruvāṉatu. (1788lēyē carvilliyam jōṉs inti mutaliya vaṭa intiya moḻikaḷiṉ ilakkaṇakkūṟukaḷ intō āriyamoḻik kuriyavaiyalla [appoḻutu “tamiḻiya moḻik kuṭumpam” paṟṟi mēṉāṭṭaṟiñarukku teriyātu] eṉṟu kūṟiyuḷḷār:

(Regarding modern Indo - Aryan Languages) : “and this analogy might induce us to believe, that the pure Hindi, whether of Tartarian or Chaldean origin, was primeval in upper India, into which the Sanskrit was introduced by conquerors from other kingdoms in some very remote age”

tamiḻiya moḻikaḷiliruntu vētamoḻi, camaṟkirutam vaṭa intiya (intō āriya moḻik kuṭumpattiṉavākak karutappaṭum) moḻikaḷ, ākiyavaṟṟil ēṟiyuḷḷa oliyaṉiyal, ilakkaṇaviyal moḻiyiyal kūṟukaḷ palavaṟṟai emaṉō tamatu 1956 kaṭṭuraiyil niṟuviṉār.  

oliyaṉiyal : ṭa;ḷa (rikvētattil), ṉa ākiya vaḷai nāvolikaḷ (retroflex/cerebral/domal tamiḻiya moḻi kaḷiliruntu kaṭaṉ peṟṟavai. avvolikaḷaik koṇṭa coṟkaḷait tamiḻiliruntu kaṭaṉ peṟṟa piṉṉar, olikaḷaiyum vaṇṇamālaiyil cērttaṉar.
viṉaiyeccam (gerund/absolutive/incomplete verb/ past nonfinite verb/converb/indeclinable participle/ adverbial participle/past participle (Jothimuthu), conjunctive participle (Grierson)  rikvēta kālattilēyē ēṟiviṭṭatu.
nēr kūṟṟu muṭintavuṭaṉ “eṉa” eṉṟu tamiḻil varuvatu pōl (“taṉceyviṉai muṭitteṉak kēṭpal-” puṟanāṉūṟu 27:9-10) camaṟkirutattil kūṟṟukkup piṉṉar “iti” iti (= ippaṭik kūṟiṉār) eṉa varutal.
etirolic coṟkaḷ (echo words ) “puli kili” pōṉṟavai
olikkuṟippuc coṟkaḷ - caḷacaḷa, paṭa paṭa pōṉṟavai.
ōṭu, ōṭṭu, ōṭṭuvi; naṭa, naṭattu, naṭattuvi pōṉṟa vāypāṭṭu (simplex, causative, causative of causative) viṉai vaṭivaṅkaḷ.
emeṉō 1974 kaṭṭurai; tamiḻ um payaṉpāṭu pōṉṟa camaṟkiruta api api ai payaṉpaṭuttutal.
mēlatu kaṭṭurai: tirāviṭa uṟavu muṟaic coṟkaḷilum cari intō āriya uṟavumuṟaic coṟkaḷilum cari perumpālum toḻilaik kuṟikkum cātippeyar āṇukku uḷḷatu. āṉāl peṇṇaik kuṟikkum poḻutu intac cātikkāraṉuṭaiya makaḷ/maṉaivi/ tāy/piṟa uṟavu eṉṟa aḷavilēyē amaintu viṭukiṟatu.

mēṟkaṇṭa ceytikaḷai kāliṉ mācikā tamatu Defining a Linguistic Area : South Asia (1976) nūlil virivāka viḷakki yuḷḷār. tamiḻiya moḻikaḷai yoṭṭiya māṟṟaṅkaḷē intō āriya moḻikaḷil ēṟiyuḷḷaṉa eṉpatu avar karuttu “(India) is a fairly stable typological area where the brunt of the burden of adaptation is borne by intrusive rather than local languages.”

pāvāṇaruṭaiya vaṭamoḻi varalāṟu (1967) 295-321 pakkaṅkaḷil ilakkaṇavatikāram eṉṉum pakutiyil piṉvarum ilakkaṇa/ moḻiyiyal kūṟukaḷai vētamoḻiyum camaṟkirutamum tamiḻiya moḻikaḷiṭamiruntē kaṭaṉpeṟṟuḷḷa eṉat teḷivāka niṟuvukiṟār.

eḻuttiyal

1. vaṇṇamālai (tamiḻiya moḻiyaiyoṭṭi uruvākkik koṇṭatu.) 2. oliyumpiṟappum 3. eḻuttuc cāriyai 4. eḻuttuvaippu muṟai, 5. eḻuttoli māttirai/
aḷapu 6. eḻuttuvaṭivam 7. puṇarcci.


colliyal

“vaṭamoḻi vēṟṟumaiyamaippum tamiḻait taḻuviyatu” “vaṭamoḻi tirimoḻiyātaliṉ ataṉ viṉaikaṭku vērc coṟkaḷ ammoḻiyil illai. avaiperumpālum iyalmoḻiyākiya tamiḻil tāṉ uḷḷaṉa.” (tātu pāṭa vērkaḷiṉ taṉmai paṟṟi ēṟkeṉevē mēlē iyal 1l, kaṇṭōm). kuṟippuc coṟkaḷ - acaikaḷ; iṇaippiṭaic col (um - untu Skt - utta; OE, Eng and) cuṭṭu/ viṉāccoṟkaḷ, viṉaiyeccam.

toṭariyal

tokaic col (samāsa); coṟṟoṭar varicai (“perumpālum tamiḻ muṟaiyai ottatē”)

The Primary Classicial Language of the World  pakkaṅkaḷ 279 - 286 l tamiḻiliruntu intō - airōppiya moḻikaḷil ēṟiyuḷḷa (muṉṉoṭṭukaḷ, piṉṉoṭṭukaḷ, cuṭṭukaḷ, viṉai īṟukaḷ mutaliya) mēlum pala moḻiyiyal, ilakkaṇak kūṟukaḷaik kuṟippiṭṭuḷḷār. 

moḻi paṭippaṭiyāka uruvāṉatā?
allatu tiṭumeṉa uruvāṉatā?

tiṭumeṉattāṉ eṉṟu 1783 l karutiyavar ṭākṭar jāṉcaṉ :

moḻi oruvar allatu oru ciṟu kuḻuviṉariṭam tiṭumeṉat tōṉṟiyirukka vēṇṭum. āyiram kuḻantaikaḷō allatu ēṉ pattu laṭcam kuḻantaikaḷ cērntō moḻiyai uruvākkiyirukka iyalātu. kuralvaḷaiyum olikkum uṟuppukaḷum ataṟkēṟṟa pakkuva nilaiyil irukkum kuḻantaip paruvattil ataṟkāṉa aṟivu kuḻantaikaḷiṭam irātu; aṟivuvaḷarvataṟkuḷ kuralvaḷaiyum piṟa uṟuppukaḷum pēccolikaḷai olikka iyalāta nilai eytiviṭum.

It must have come by inspiration. A thousand, nay a million of children could not invent a language. While the organs are pliable there is not understanding enough to form a language; by the time there is understanding enough the organs are become stiff.
hāri hāyjar karuttum atuvē: [moḻikku muntaiya nilaiyiliruntu (olikkuṟippukaḷ pōṉṟavai) moḻi tōṉṟiyataṉāl] mānta iṉa varalāṟṟil niṉaittuppārkka iyalāta eṇṇiṟanta puraṭcikaramāṉa māṟṟaṅkaḷ nikaḻntaṉa. attakaiya uruvākkam orē oru muṟai tāṉ orē iṭattil tāṉ nikaḻntirukka iyalum. uruvāṉatum atu kaṭum viraivil māntarkaḷ aṉaivariṭamum paraviyirukkum atāvatu ñāla mutaṉmoḻi ōriṭattil oru muṟaiuruvāṉatē; mānta marapiyaliṉ paṭi aṉaittu māntarum uṭaṉ piṟappiṉar, atē pōl mutaṉmoḻiyaṭippaṭaiyilum uṭaṉpiṟappukaḷē yāvar.”

[The cirucumstances under which pre - language could have become language are] so far - reaching. so revolutionary and so fruitful as to suggest that it was created only once and in only in one place. Once created. it would have spread to all hominids irresistibly and speedily. It suggests, in other words the unitary origin of language, and the unitary origin of language suggests in turn,. that men are cultural as well as genetic and biological brothers Harry Hoijer (1969) “The origin of language”.

ñālamutaṉmoḻi oṉṟē eṉpatai valiyuṟutti māris cuvātecu 1972 l The Origin and Diversification of Language eṉṟa virivāṉa nūl eḻutiyuḷḷār.

ār.em. ṭapiḷyū. ṭikcaṉ The rise and fall of languages (1977) nūlil ñāla mutaṉmoḻitiṭumeṉa, veṭi veṭittatu pōlat (like an explosion) tōṉṟiyirukkum eṉkiṟār. mānta iṉa mūḷaivaḷarcci takka takuti peṟṟa nilaiyil māntamūḷai tiṭumeṉak kaṭṭukkōppāṉa oru muḻu amaippāka moḻiyait tōṟṟuvittirukkum eṉkiṟār(The human brain would have invented it, almost as a complete system; “Language would have burst forth”) tamiḻ oṉṟē iyalmoḻi eṉṟum eṉavē ammoḻiyil tāṉ coṟkaḷ (ēṉ piṟa moḻic coṟkaḷum) aṉaittum cila aṭippaṭai vērkaḷiliruntu ēraṇa muṟaippaṭi paṭippaṭiyāka aṟivupūrvamāka ēṟkattakka vakaiyil vaḷarntu peruki vantuḷḷataṉai meyppikka muṭiyum eṉṟa pāvāṇar kōṭpāṭṭukku ṭikcaṉ karuttu oraḷavu ātaravu taruvataik kāṇalām. [“iyalmoḻi eṉṟu pāvāṇar kūṟiyatu mānta iṉa mutal tāymoḻikku mika neruṅkiyatu tamiḻ eṉṟa
karuttil.]”
10. moḻi tiṭumeṉa uruvākiyatāyiṉ. ñāṉap pirakācar (1927) mutaliṟ kūṟiyatum pāvāṇar(1943,1950,1953,1967 nūl pakkam 70-83 tiṭpamāka virivāka vaṭivamaittatum āṉa cuṭṭaṭik koḷkaiyāṉatu ñāṉamutal moḻi āyvāḷar karututaṟku uriyatu. tamiḻc coṟkaḷuḷ mukkāṟ pakuti cuṭṭaṭic coṟkaḷiliruntu uruvākiyatu eṉpār pāvāṇar (1950). cuṭṭaṭik koḷkaiyai mikac curukkamāka piṉvarumāṟu taralām.

(ā - ī - ū) ----------------   (  a - i - u)
a. cēymaik karuttuc coṟkaḷ
i. aṉmai, piṉmai, iḻikai
u (muṉmaic cuṭṭu) ul maṟṟum ukarattuṭaṉ k,c,t,n,p,m ākiya meykaḷaic cērttu(Plenary operation of the principle of consonental prosthesis) uruvākum ul, kul, cul, tul, (nul), pul, mul ākiya mūla vērkaḷil iruntu tōṉṟal (muṉvarutal) muṉmai, muṟcellal (cellutal), neruṅkal, (ceṟittal, kūṭutal); poruntutal (kūṭutal, ottal), vaḷaital, vaḷaittal, turuvutal ākiya eṭṭup peruṅkaruttukkaḷum. (ivaṟṟiṟku iṭaippaṭṭaṉavum ivaṟṟiṟkiḷaittaṉavumāṉa) ettuṇaiyō nuṇkaruttukkaḷum avaṟṟiṟkāṉa eṇṇiṟanta tamiḻc coṟkaḷum avaṟṟiṉ paṉmaṭittiripukaḷāṉa palvēṟu “kuṭumpa” moḻic coṟkaḷum piṟakkiṉṟaṉa eṉpār avar. ikkoḷkaiyiṉ virivaip pāvāṇar mutaltāymoḻi (1953) nūlil tantuḷḷār. 1979 l pāvāṇar eḻutiya “An epitome of the Lemurian Language and its ramifications”eṉṉum āṅkilak kaṭṭuraiyil ikkaruttiṉ āṅkilac curukkattai piṉvarumāṟu tantuḷḷār:

“The three fundamental ideas, viz., remoteness, proximity, and frontality (or forwardness) expressed by the three demonstrative vowels have generated a million words which go to make up Tamil, and have also produced subsequently and genealogically a confused multiplicity of dependant languages of varying degrees of richness and importance, through the process of mutation and derivation.... Of the words contained in any language, the greatest majority owe their origin to the idea of frontwardness.”

ec. es. ṭēviṭ (1966) kaṭṭuraikaḷil kuṟippiṭum 21 aṭippaṭait tamiḻvērkaḷum (vaḷ/vaṇ, uḷ (uṇ) uṇṭu, kēḷ/ kēḷir, kēṇmai, koḷ/ koṭu/kōṭal; kīḻ; pōḻ; ol, kal, kāl, cāl, nil, pāl, palcil, tol val, eḷ, oḷ, viḷ) avai cārnta coṟṟokutikaḷum oru pāṉmai pāvāṇar karuttōṭu ottu nōkkattakkaṉa vākum.

māntaṉmoḻi (ēṟattāḻa 50000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar) orē iṭattil tiṭumeṉat tōṉṟiyirukkalāmeṉṟum atilikuntu paṭippaṭiyāka urumāṟit tōṉṟiyavaiyē iṉṟuḷḷa 6000 moḻikaḷum eṉakkarutum aṟiñar karuttukaḷai mēlē kaṇṭōm. inta iṭattil tamiḻakattil palarum iṉṟu ceppivarum oruātāramaṟṟa kūṟṟaik kuṟippiṭa vēṇṭum. atu eṉṉa? nōvām cāmaskiyum ‘ulaka moḻikaḷ aṉaittiṟkum tamiḻmoḻiyē tāyāka irukkakkūṭum eṉṟu kūṟiviṭṭār’ eṉpatē atu. appaṭi avar kūṟavē illai. kūṟakkūṭiyavarum allar. camaṇamuṉivar ūṉ cōṟu ceytār eṉpatu pōla uḷḷatu cāmski paṟṟiya ikkūṟṟu!’avar āyvuppulam vēṟu; māntaṉiṭam enta uḷaviyal - uṭaliyal aṭippaṭaiyil moḻi āṟṟal tōṉṟiyirukkalām eṉpatē avar āyvup pulamākum; varalāṟṟu moḻiyiyal pakkamō, ñāla mutaṉmoḻi etuvāka irukkalām eṉpatu paṟṟiyō vaṇṇaṉai moḻiyiyalaṟiñarāṉa avar celpavarallar. mēlum cāmski pōṉṟōr tākkattiṉāl tāṉ varalāṟṟu moḻiyiyal cārnta āyvukaḷiṉ kaṭanta muppatu nāṟpatu āṇṭukaḷil ñāla mutaṉmoḻi āyvukaḷ pōṉṟavaṟṟil tēkka nilai ēṟpaṭṭatu eṉpatai viḷakkuvar āṇṭrūs kārsṭārs - makkārti pōṉṟōr. tamiḻil iruntu ulakamoḻikaḷ aṉaittum tōṉṟiṉa eṉṟu cāmski kūṟiṉār eṉṟa piḻaicceyti eppaṭip paraviyatu. 22.11.2001 Frontline  itaḻil cāmski kalkattāvil pēciyatu veḷivantatu. āṅkila aṟivu nirampāta varum moḻiyiyal paṟṟit teriyātavarum āṉaoru aṉpar ōmcakti ēpral 2002 itaḻil antap pēccait tamiḻil tamiḻākkam ceyta poḻutu ippiḻaik karutatai acciṭṭār cāmsaki pēcciṉ cariyāṉa tamiḻākkamum āṅkila mūlamum aṭuttuta tarappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. valuvāṉa ātāraṅkaḷ irukkum poḻutu kaṟpaṉaic ceytikaḷaik kūṟuvatu kaṉiyiruppak kāykavar vatākum. [ulakil uḷḷa 6000 moḻikaḷil 3340 moḻikaḷil ovvoṉṟaiyum pēcuvōr pattāyirattukkumkuṟaivāṉavarkaḷē. avaṟṟuḷ 3000 moḻikaḷ 2100kkuḷ aḻintuviṭum eṉṟu moḻiyiyalaṟiñar karutukiṉṟaṉar. itu teriyāmal “aḻivuṟum nilaiyil uḷḷa moḻikaḷ paṭṭiyalil 6 kōṭi makkaḷ pēcum tamiḻaiyum yuṉeskō cērttuḷḷatu” eṉṟa pētaimaik karuttaip palarum parappalāyiṉar. itu muḻut tavaṟu eṉpatu. toṉmaic cemmoḻi tamiḻ 2007 nūlil iṟuti iyalil teḷivākkap paṭṭuḷḷatu. atu pōlavēcāmaski paṟṟiya piḻaikkaruttaiyum teḷivākkuvatu iṉṟiyamaiyātatu. cāmskiyiṭam yārāvatu eḻutik kēṭṭāl ‘eṉṉatu? nāṉā? eppoḻutu coṉṉēṉ” eṉṟu allavā kūṟi nakaippar.]

ulakamoḻikaḷ aṉaittiṟkum tamiḻē tāy moḻiyāka irukkakkūṭum eṉṟu iṉṟuvarai mēṉāṭṭu moḻiyiyalaṟiñar evarum kūṟavillai. kālṭuvel 1856/1875l kūṟiyatum ñālamutaṉmoḻikkut tol tamiḻ (tirāviṭam) mika neruṅkiyatāka irukkalām eṉpatē. (vivaraṅkaḷē mēlē kaṇṭōm) ñāla mutaṉmoḻi tamiḻ eṉṟa mākaṟal kārttikēya mutaliyār, ñāṉappirakācar, tēvanēyap pāvāṇar koḷkai iṉi ulakil viraivil niṟuvap paṭalām. -- iḷaiñarāṉa moḻiyiyalaṟiñar (tamiḻōṭu ēṉaiya kuṭumpamoḻi oṉṟiraṇṭil āḻnta pulamai peṟṟavar) mēlai nāṭukaḷil iṉṟu ivvāyvukaḷil īṭupaṭuvōruṭaṉ oruṅkiṇaintu pāvāṇar vaḻiyil āyvuceytāl. 

nōvām cāmski 22.11.01 aṉṟu kalkattāp palkalaikkaḻakattil āṟṟiya urai [āṅkila mūlam

Frontline  21.12.01] moḻi paṟṟiya aṟiviyal uṇmaikaḷ.

(“kalkattāp palkalaik kaḻakam tarum”) inta muṉaivar paṭṭattai intiyāvil peṟuvatu eṉakkup perumakiḻcci tarukiṟatu. intiyāvil tāṉ nāṉ cārntirukkum “moḻiyiyal” eṉṉum viññāṉap pulattil kaṇicamāṉa pakuti cumār 2000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar uruvākkap paṭṭatu eṉpatu uṅkaḷukkum teriyum. ippoḻutu nām ‘toṭariyal ilakkaṇam’ (generative grammar) eṉṟuaḻaikkum vakaiyaic cārnta mutalāvatu ilakkaṇameṉṟu oraḷavukkuk kūṟattakkatu pāṇiṉiyiṉ
camcukkiruta ilakkaṇam tāṉ. 50 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉ eṉatu putiya ‘toṭariyal ilakkaṇak koḷkaiyai nāṉ niṟuviya poḻutu pāṇiṉiyattilum itaiyotta cila karuttukkaḷ iruntatu teriyātu, antapputukkoḷkai moḻiyiyalil vērūṉṟiya piṉṉar tāṉ paḻaiya ilakkaṇa marapukaḷilum irunta atupōṉṟa karuttukkaḷ iṉam kāṇappaṭṭu iṉṟaiya moḻiyiyalil viññāṉa aṭippaṭaiyil putu viyākkiyāṉaṅkaḷ tarappaṭṭaṉa. paṇṭaiya ilakkaṇac celvaṅkaḷ palavaṟṟil (pāṇiṉīyam uṭpaṭa) ikkaruttukkaḷ aṭaiyāḷam kāṇappaṭṭaṉa. eṉiṉum appaḻaiya nūlkaḷiṉ kūṟṟukkaḷil iṉṉum teḷivu peṟa vēṇṭiyaṉa pala uḷḷaṉa; mēlum ārāycci ceytāl naṟpayaṉ kiṭṭum. 
itaṟkiṭaiyil taṟkāla moḻiyiyal āyvukaḷ taṉittuvamāṉa pātaiyil celkiṉṟaṉa. maṉitaṉiṉ uṭaṟkūṟṟiyal cūḻaliṉ oru paṅkākavē moḻiyai avvāyvukaḷ karutukiṉṟaṉa. “uṭaṟkūṟṟiyalcār nōkku” (biolinguistic approach) eṉpatu itaṟku oru peyar ākum. uṭaliṉ, kuṟippāka mūḷaiyiṉ oru āṟṟal eṉṟē moḻi karutappaṭukiṟatu. kaṇkaḷiṉ pārkkum āṟṟal, tacai narampukaḷiṉ iyaṅkiyal āṟṟal pōṉṟatutāṉ mūḷaiyiṉ moḻi āṟṟal eṉpatē innōkku.

maṉita iṉattukkēyuriya “moḻi āṟṟal” iraṇṭu vakaiyil taṉittaṉmai peṟṟatu. 1.atu maṉita iṉattil aṉaivarukkum oru cīrāka amaintuḷḷatu. 2. uyiriṉaṅkaḷil maṉitaṉukku maṭṭumē moḻi uriyatu.

ciṟappiyalpu I cila āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar varai caṟṟu viyappuṭaṉēyē karutappaṭṭu vantatu. ippoḻutu appaṭi alla. moḻi āṟṟalil maṉita iṉattiṉ palvēṟu pirivukaḷukkuḷ entavēṟupāṭum illai eṉpatai aṇmai āyvukaḷ kāṭṭu kiṉṟaṉa. iṉṟaikkuc cumār 1,00,000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar irunta āṇ- peṇ tampatiyariṉ ( allatu oru cila āṭavar – makaḷir kuḻuviṉ) vāricukaḷē iṉṟu ulakil vāḻum aṉaittu (600 kōṭi) maṉitarkaḷum eṉṟa muṭivaip potuvāka ellā viññāṉikaḷum ēṟkiṉṟaṉar. itiliruntu eṉṉa terikiṟatu? vaḷamāṉa ceṟivāṉa moḻittiṟaṉai aṭaivataṟkāṉa iyalpāṉa āṟṟal aṉaivarukkum camamākavē amaintuḷḷatu. avvāṟṟalil vēṟupāṭu irukkumāyiṉ atu mikamikac ciṟiya vēṟupāṭēyākum. iṉṟuḷḷa muṟaikaḷiṉ paṭi avvēṟupāṭṭaik kaṇṭupiṭikkavē muṭiyātu.

ciṟappiyalpu II viyappiṟkuriyatu. maṉitaṉ tavira vēṟu enta uyiriṉattukkum “moḻi” yeṉṟu kuṟippiṭattakka āṟṟal illavē illai. oru cila pūcciyiṉaṅkaḷiṭaiyē (tēṉī, eṟumpu) maṭṭum moḻiyiṉ taṉmaiyil oru nuṇṇiya paṅku oruvēḷai irukkalām. maṉitaṉ tavira ovvoru uyiriṉamum tattamakkuḷ toṭarpu koḷḷum muṟaikaḷaip paṭṭiyaliṭṭāl atil “moḻi” illai! “moḻi eṉpatu aṭippaṭaiyil ceytip parimāṟṟak karuvi” eṉṟa potuvāṉa nampikkaiyum ātāram aṟṟatēyākum.

maṉita iṉattiṉ piṟa taṉmaikaḷaip pōl “moḻi” eṉpataiyum paramparai marapiyal(genetics) tāṉ nirṇayikkiṟatu. avaravar vāḻkkai aṉupavattiṟkēṟṟapaṭi moḻittiṟaṉ vaḷarcci vēṟupaṭalā meṉiṉum vēṟupāṭukaḷ varampukkuṭpaṭṭavai. maṉitaṉākap piṟantavuṭaṉēyē aṉaivarukkum moḻittiṟaṉai vaḷarttuk koḷḷum aṭippaṭai āṟṟal. orē mātiri amaintuviṭukiṟatu. eṉavē tāṉ “vēṟupāṭukaḷ varampukkuṭpaṭṭavai” eṉṟa nilai. maṉitaṉiṉ moḻikaḷ (nēṟṟaiya, iṉṟaiya, nāḷaiya moḻikaḷ) aṉaittum ēṟattāḻa orē accil vārttavaiyē. nām vilaṅkukaḷaip paṟṟi āyvu ceykiṟōm. cevvāyk kiraka uyiriyāṉa (cevvāyil uyiriṉamē kiṭaiyātu! nām colvatu kaṟpaṉai uyiri!) oṉṟu iṅku vantu maṉitaṉai āyvu ceytāl “maṉita iṉam muḻuvatum orē moḻiyaittāṉ pēcukiṟatu - cila vēṟupāṭukaḷuṭaṉ” eṉṟu tāṉ kūṟum. nam moḻikaḷiṭaiyē uḷḷa vēṟupāṭukaḷ aṉṟāṭa vāḻkaiyil mukkiyam āṉavai. āṉāl avaiyaṉaittiṭamum uḷḷa aṭippaṭai orumaippāṭṭai (atu eṉṉa eṉṟu putti pūrvamākap purintu koḷḷāviṭṭālum) uṇarvu pūrvamāka uṭkoṇṭutāṉ ellā maṉitarum ceyalpaṭukiṉṟaṉar. moḻikaḷaik kaṟpikkap payaṉpaṭum marapilakkaṇaṅkaḷum akaramutalikaḷum moḻikku moḻi uḷḷa taṟceyalāka vāytta vēṟupāṭukaḷaic cuṭṭik kāṭṭukiṉṟaṉa. avaṟṟiṉ nōkkattil atu caritāṉ. viññāṉa muṟaiyil moḻiyai āyvu ceyvatil oṉṟuk koṉṟu toṭarpuṭaiya iraṇṭu amcaṅkaḷ uḷḷaṉa. 1. eṉṟum māṟāta oli, poruḷ, ilakkaṇa amaippu variccaṭṭaṅkaḷmaṉita mūḷaiyiṉ amaippilēyē nirṇayikkappaṭṭu viṭukiṉṟaṉa. 2. ovvoruvarum tam aṉupavattiṟkēṟpa eytum moḻittiṟaṉum (ciṟcila vēṟupāṭukaḷ iruppiṉum) anta māṟāta nirṇayattiṟkuḷ tāṉ vērūṉṟi irukkum.

maṉitaṉiṉ mūḷaiyil moḻittiṟaṉ māṟāta vaṇṇam nirṇayikkappaṭṭu viṭṭa nilaiyil ciṟu aḷavilāṉa māṟu pāṭukaḷukku maṭṭumē vāyppu (options) uṇṭu. ivaṟṟiṉ taṉmaikaḷai āyvu ceytapiṉ ceyya vēṇṭiyatu māṟupāṭṭu vāyppu oṉṟait tērnteṭuttāl oru moḻiyum, vēṟu oṉṟait tērnteṭuttāl vēṟu oru moḻiyum oru maṉitaṉiṉ moḻiyāka amaintuviṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉṟu kāṭṭuvatutāṉ. oru iṭattil, oru kālattil avvāyppu tamiḻ eṉṟa moḻiyāka amaintuviṭukiṟatu. kaṟārākac coṉṉāl tamiḻiṉ vaṭṭāra vaḻakkukaḷil oṉṟu eṉac colla vēṇṭum [atāvatu tamiḻnāṭṭil piṟakkum kuḻantai tamiḻ pēcukiṟatu; atuvum vaṭṭārattukkut takkavāṟu attamiḻiṉ taṉmaiyum vēṟupaṭukiṟatu eṉṟu colla varukiṟār cāmski]. iṉṉoru iṭam, kālattil avvāyppu “cuvāhili” moḻi ākiviṭukiṟatu. ēṉaiya moḻikaḷukkum avvāṟē kūṟik koḷka. intat tattuvattai vēṟu coṟkaḷil piṉvarumāṟu kūṟulām. maṉita moḻiyiṉ nirṇayikkappaṭṭu viṭṭa aṭippaṭai, ciṟu ciṟu māṟupāṭṭu vāyppukaḷ, ivaṟṟiṉ aṭippaṭaiyil ovvoru moḻiyilum ettaṉai vākkiyaṅkaḷaiyum col – coṟṟoṭar kaḷaiyum amaikkalām? avaṟṟiṉ  li, poruḷ eṉṉa? karuttait terivikka, takavalkōra, katai colla, pōṉṟa pala kāriyaṅkaḷukku ententa vaḻikaḷil ammoḻiyil vākkiyaṅkaḷaiyum coṟ ṟoṭarkaḷaiyum amaikkac cāttiyamuṇṭu? ivaṟṟaik kaṇṭu piṭippatē moḻiyiyaliṉ vēlai. itu eḷitalla. kaṭiṉamāṉatu. varuṅkālattil pala talaimuṟai āyvāḷarkaḷiṉ āṟṟalum muyaṟciyum ivvāyvukkut tēvai eṉpatil aiyamillai. eṉiṉum āyvu vaḷarcci ūkkam taruvatāka uḷḷatu. kaṭanta 20 āṇṭukaḷil māṟupaṭṭa ilakkaṇakkaṭṭamaippuk koṇṭa moḻikaḷ palavaṟṟaip paṟṟiya uṇmaikaḷ pala teriyavantuḷḷaṉa. muṉṉarē āyvukkuṭpaṭuttappaṭṭa (camacukkirutam pōṉṟa) moḻikaḷ aḷavukku, immoḻikaḷilāṉa putiya āyvukaḷum āḻamākanaṭantuḷḷaṉa. viyattaku moḻiyiyal uṇmaikaḷ kaṇṭupiṭikkappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. niṉaittuk kūṭap pārkkāta viṉākkaḷ eḻuntuḷḷaṉa. palavaṟṟukkup poruttamāṉa viṭaikaḷ tōṉṟip putiya ārāyccip pātaikaḷukku vaḻi vakukkappaṭṭuḷḷatu. 

aṇmaiyil tōṉṟiyuḷḷa oru āyvuppulattil eṉakkum ārvam uṇṭu. moḻiyāṉatu laṭciya vaṭivamaippu (optional design) koṇṭatā? eṉpatu atu.puṟaccūḻnilaiyiṉ nipantaṉaikaḷukkuṭpaṭṭē moḻiyaip payaṉpaṭutta muṭiyum. (e.kā. nēraṭiyākavō karuvi mūlamākavō uṇarvuppulaṉukku Sensorimotor system eṭṭuvatāka moḻi irukka vēṇṭum) annipantaṉaikaḷukkut tīrvāka amaiyak kūṭiyatāka enta aḷavukku maṉitaṉiṉ moḻi irukkiṟatu eṉpatē inta āyvuppulam. “iyaṟkai muḻumaipeṟṟatu” eṉṟu uḷḷuṇarvu pūrvamāka uṇarntu coṉṉāṉ kalīliyō. moḻi itaṟkup  porunti varukiṟatā eṉa nirūpikkumāṟu nām kēṭkalām. navīṉa viññāṉak karuttukkaḷ palavaṟṟilum inta uḷḷuṇarvuk karuttu vaḻi kāṭṭiyāka iruppatāl itai nirūpikkum paṇi viññāṉikaḷ mēṟkoḷḷa vēṇṭiya paṇitāṉ. aṟpa uyiriṉaṅkaḷaip poṟuttavaraiyil attakaiya viṉākkaḷukkuc cila viṭaikaḷ  kaṇṭupiṭikkappaṭṭuḷṉa. vairas nuṇkirumikaḷiṉ puṟapparappu ‘pala camapakkappaṭṭai vaṭivam’ (polyhedral) koṇṭatu etaṉāl eṉpataik kaṇṭupiṭittu viṭṭaṉar. moḻiyiṉ taṉmaikaḷukkum itu pōṉṟa viḷakkam kiṭaikkalām eṉṟu nampakkūṭiya āyvukaḷ naṭaipeṟṟu varukiṉṟaṉa. uyiriṉappariṇāma vaḷarcciyil
kaṭaiciyāka aṇmaiyil tōṉṟiyavaṉ maṉitaṉ. ammaṉita iṉattil maṭṭumē tōṉṟiyuḷḷa (maṉita uṭal - uḷaviyal veḷip pāṭāṉa) moḻiyāṉatu maṉita iyalpilum vāḻvilum maiyappakuti vakukkiṟatu. itu cārnta aṇmai āyvuk kaṇṭupiṭippukaḷ pala poruttamāṉavaiyāka uḷḷaṉa. avai potuvāka uyiriṉap pariṇāma vaḷarcci kuṟittum kuṟippāka maṉita iṉam kuṟittum pala viṉākkaḷai eḻuppukiṉṟaṉa. moḻi, atai maṉitaṉ eytippayaṉ paṭuttuma muṟaikaḷ, maṉita vāḻvil ataṉ paṅku paṟṟiyellām neṭuṅkālamāka eḻuntu vantuḷḷa pala piracciṉaikaḷukku ittakaiya   āyvukaḷ putiya viyattaku tīrvukaḷait tantuḷḷaṉa.

moḻipaṟṟiyum mūḷaiyiṉ ēṉaiya uyarnilaic ceyal pāṭukaḷ paṟṟiyum āṉa āyvukaḷil varum āṇṭukaḷil paraparappāṉa āyvu muṭivukaḷ veḷivaralām. iṉṟu nuṭpamāṉa cikkalāṉa viññāṉattuṟaiyāka vaḷarntuvarum ‘moḻiyiyal’ āyvukaḷil mukkiyamāka āyvup pātaikaḷ cilavaṟṟukkup paṇṭaik kālattilēyē kālkōḷ ceyyappaṭṭa inta (intiya) nāṭṭaiviṭa vēṟu enta iṭam poruttamāṉatu ivvāyvukḷait toṭara! eṉṉaip poṟutta varai ittakaiya moḻiyiyal āyvukaḷaik kūrntu nōkkavum, avaṟṟil paṅku peṟavum ārvamāṉa etirpārppuṭaṉ nāṉ kāttirukkiṟēṉ.
 
Understanding Human Language

Address by professor Noam chonsky at the special convocation of the University of Calcutta held on November 22, 2001 to award the degree D.Litt - Honoris Causa.

I would like to express my gratitude at being welcomed into this distinguished intellectual community, with its vibrant and rich tradition. It is particularly gratifying to receive this honour in India. My professional  field, as I am sure you know, was in large part created in India, 2,500 years ago. The first “generative grammar” in something like the modern sense is Panini’s grammar of Sanskrit. Nothing was known about these similarities at the origins of the modern versions 50 years ago. It was only after the modern field had taken shape that earlier tradition, long forgotten, began to be explored and reinterpreted in the light of recent insights. Many treasures were discovered, among them Panini’s classic – though crucial issues of interpretation remain obscure, and there are surely research topics that could prove highly rewarding. 

Meanwhile contemporary inquiries proceeded along their own distinctive course. Characteristically, they view language in a biological setting, adopting what is sometimes called a “biolinguistic approach”. From this point of view, the human faculty of language is regarded basically as an organ of the body, mostly the brain, more or less on a par with the visual system or the system of motor organization.

The language faculty is a “species property” in a dual sense. First, it is close to uniform for the species; second, it is apparently unique to humans in essentials. 

The first of these properties is less surprising than it seemed a few years ago. Recent studies indicate that there is remarkably little genetic variation within the human species, so little that it is now commonly assumed that contemporary humans are all descendants of a very small breeding group, perhaps about 100,000 years ago, a conclusion that has broad implications, With regard to language, apart from the margins, variation in the capacity to acquire a rich and highly articulated knowledge of language is so slight as to be virtually undetectable, at least by present means.

The uniqueness property is more surprising. There are no known analogues to human language elsewhere in the animal world. The closest analogies, and these are very weak, are remote: perhaps in some species of insects. Human language does not even find a place in standard taxonomies of animal communication systems; and in fact, there is no strong reason to think of it as primarily a system of communication, contrary to common belief.

Language is like other biological systems, however, in that its basic character is genetically determined. Each person, of course, undergoes a specific course of development, shaped by individual experience, but in highly limited ways. The outcomes are largely a result of shared initial endowment. The human languages, existing or possible, are pretty much cast to the same mould. A rational Martian scientist, studying humans the way we study other animals, could reasonably conclude that there really is only one language, with only minor variations. The variations are very important for our lives; the far deeper uniformities we simply take for granted, without awareness. Similarly, traditional and pedagogical grammars and dictionaries are concerned with the unpredictable and somewhat accidental variation, rightly for their special purposes. The interests of the scientific study of language are virtually complementary: the invariant principles of sound, meaning, and structure that are  rooted in our mental nature and that determine the fundamental nature of the languages that each person comes to acquire under normal circumstances. 

One basic problem, then, is to discover the invariant principles of the language faculty and the limited options of variation, and then to show that the possible human languages are determined by selecting among the options: one choice yields Tamil (more exactly, a specific variety of Tamil) another yields Swahili, etc. Putting it differently, the task is to show that with a specific choice of options by adhering to the fixed principles one can literally deduce the infinite array of expressions of language; their sound, their meaning, the ways in which they can be used to express thoughts, to request information, to tell stories, and numerous others. The task is immensely challenging and difficult. It will doubtless occupy the efforts and energies of many generations of researchers. Nevertheless, there has been quite encouraging progress. In the past 20 years particularly, there has been a flood of discoveries about languages of virtually every known typological variety. Like the well – studied languages, these have been investigated in far greater depth than ever before, revealing many entirely unexpected properties. New questions have arisen that had never been envisioned before. In many cases there have also been plausible answers, sometimes opening new directions for inquiry. 

One novel question that has come to the fore in recent years, and that happens to be of particular interest to me, is the question of “optimal design”. To what extent is human language an optimal solution to externally – imposed conditions that language much satisfy to be usable at all (for example, accessibility to sensori motor systems). Equivalently, we may ask to what extent language satisfies the Galilean intuition that “nature is perfect” and it is the task of the scientist to prove it, a guiding intuition for the modern sciences.

There are some answers to such questions for very simple organisms: for example, an explanation of the fact that the shells to viruses are polyhedral. Current work suggests that something similar may be true for human language, a biological system that has emerged in the last moment of evolutionary time, in the most complex organism known, and is surely at the core of our nature and life. Such conclusions, which by now have considerable substance, raise many questions about biological evolution and development generally, and about the human species in particular. These research efforts have also provided new and often surprising solutions to long- standing problems,\ of language, its acquisition and use, and its place in the biological world.

There should be very exciting years ahead in the study of language and other higher mental faculties. There is no better place to pursue these questions than in the land that was the original home of some of the major strands of inquiry that are now being woven into a most intriguing fabric. Speaking personally, I look forward with much eagerness and anticipation to observing, and participating in, these very promising developments.
Front line, December 21, 2001.
